Added three pairs to the school ladder: equal-part pictures for a half, a quarter and three quarters, matched to their names. Each card names the fraction aloud when turned; the longest label is two lines. The school range now ends at 11. The level-relative how-to, game inventory, GAMES entry and roadmap are updated. The art is code-drawn; the existing id-based ghost finger can use the three-pair deck.

- `npm run typecheck` passed using the sibling checkout's installed dependencies. Rule tests, browser play, screenshot review and the ghost-finger demonstration were not run.
- Not established: actual legibility on a device, device speech, iPad touch feel or a child's response.

### Fill and Dump (2026-10-10)

The first of the gap review's new games, built from its sketch (roadmap chunk 4): a lap and toddler game in Counting Cove, `src/games/fill-dump/`, region `counting-cove`, bands lap 1–2 and toddler 1–4. *Sketch as written:* "Fun action: tip a basket and watch fruit tumble out with a chime for each piece; drag or tap pieces back in. Skill: containment and capacity. Smallest round: a big basket and a bin; any dump is a success; the round ends when everything is back in or after a few dumps. Support: lap has no wrong move; toddler fills a basket with a stated number; preschool tips a jug to a line, where the flow slows near the line and an overfill spills into a tray that can be poured back; a glow marks the line after two overshoots. Deeper play: compare two cups, "which holds more?", pour one into the other. Co-play: the grown-up dumps and she fills."

*Built:* taps only (a one-year-old will not drag), no bin (the grass is the bin), and the "stated number" levels end on a green check rather than on reaching the number, because a game that stops the moment the count is right has no wrong move and the number would carry no decision. Levels 1–2 tip out and scoop (3 pieces once, then 4 pieces twice); levels 3–4 put in one to three of five, then three to five of seven. The rules are a reducer in `logic.ts` with tests: any taps at all finish levels 1–2 with no miss; a ghost finger finishes levels 3–4 from any state in at most pieces + 1 taps; landing places are separate cells of a grid at least 128 units apart in four view shapes. A wrong check is the only miss; every second miss glows a fruit to add, the basket to take one out of, or the check. **Pour slice built:** see [the pour-mode note](#fill-and-dump-pour-mode-2026-10-10). "Which holds more?" and Quick Tricks' third show remain proposed.

### Garden Grow levels 6–7: One more and two rows of three (2026-10-10)

Added a pre-K window at levels 6–7. Level 6 asks for one more flower than the pictured sign; level 7 presents six flowers in two rows of three and six garden beds in the same arrangement. The child can remove planted seeds and try again, and two misses glow the sign and the beds still to fill. Seeded rule tests cover both prompts and the bot's complete routes. Typecheck and 42 focused Garden Grow, registry, how-to and land tests passed; the filtered level-7 play passed with a hint and saved reward; its clean ghost finger finished in 38.8 seconds. The level-7 screenshot was reviewed. No build or full suite; iPad target feel, device speech and child play remain open.

### Mail Carrier level 8: Which way is shorter? (2026-10-10)

Added a school round after the picture-map deliveries: identify the addressed house, compare the two loop paths marked with stepping stones, and choose the shorter route for the pet to walk. Every generated destination has a short and long simple path separated by at least two stones; the two displayed choices show the count. A wrong destination or path is gentle, and two route misses light the shorter choice. The level has its own spoken instruction, scoped how-to and ghost-finger demo. Typecheck, eight rule tests, the filtered level-8 play and its 22.3-second ghost-finger demo passed. No full unit run or build; iPad, device speech and child play remain open.

### Clap the Syllables level 5: Long words (2026-10-10)

Added a school round that sorts eight picture words into one-, two-, three- and four-clap bins. Watermelon and helicopter extend the pictured word pool to four syllables; a seeded picker fills all four bins without repeating a word, and two rows keep the eight 140-unit cards apart. The level opens a school window at level 5 while preserving the earlier preschool and pre-K ranges. Typecheck, 13 focused logic tests, the five-level `clap` browser suite and the level-5 ghost-finger demo (24.8 seconds) passed. Screenshots of the level and portrait layout were reviewed. iPad target feel, device speech clarity and child play remain open.

### Where we were (2026-10-09)

The "Where we are" bullets before they were shortened. The playtest narrative in the couch bullet is the part that was cut.

- **74 games** across five places on an age trail that climbs in band order: Puddle Lagoon (lap, 37 games), Daisy Meadow (toddler, 48), Bumpy Hills (preschool, 59), Starry Peak (pre-K, 69) and Wonder Woods (early school, ages 6–8, 52). The newest is **Habitat Helpers**, a small food/water/shelter garden model (below). Every game has rule tests, a grown-up how-to card and adaptive levels; the [README](../../README.md#the-island-and-its-games) lists them.
- **The child's island:** pet hatching and customization, a sticker book, a gold twinkle on unplayed games, a favorites shelf of hearted games, a **how-to card every time a game opens** (its goal, how this level plays (held arrows beside "this level" step to another level in her band) and how the round ends on a full-screen card with a big Play and Back, and a bot-played demonstration window (a ghost finger, or a mouse pointer on a desktop, for 45 games so far, all fifteen couch games among them); a grown-up can turn it off, tapping "again" after a round skips it, and the hold-to-open `?` still shows the card in a round), and two place layouts (the swipe path and subject cards) still to compare on the iPad.
- **One story:** the Windy Picnic, with a journal, resumable steps and a keepsake.
- **The treehouse:** six free furnishings, a framed sticker, a picture board for a kept Stamp Studio picture, Rainbow Fingers painting or Pixel Pictures design, a tune plaque for a free Song Maker song, and a discovery journal of twenty-five observations from Sink or Float, Animal Snack, Photo Safari, Seesaw Balance, Bouncy Launch and Habitat Helpers.
- **Couch play:** twenty-one controller-ready games (fifteen from the island, and **Sudoku Garden**, **Lantern Lights**, **Picture Logic**, **Word Search**, **Island Bridges** and **Pond Conga**, which exist only on the couch), six-stop trips in Together or Face-off mode, how-to screens with bot demos, games that open as trips are finished, a finale and keepsake, thirteen challenge courses (Pond Practice, the Five Ponds, Cloud Hopper, Sudoku Garden's Six Beds and Three Big Beds, Lantern Lights' Dusk on the Pond, Picture Logic's Pond Pictures and The Big Pictures, Ferry Jam's Busy Harbors, Word Search's Pond Words and The Big Hunt, Island Bridges' Island Hopping, and Pond Conga's Crumb Trail) with per-player records, a **Settings** page (where she is playing, couch sound, text size, who is playing, names, backup), and a **Just me** mode with a puzzle shelf (each course's best against par) and a replay of Penguin Slide's best route. In its first playtest (2026-10-07) the courses were the fun part, and Penguin Slide's restart did not work: the couch screen takes no pointer input, so the round arrow beside the pond (it only ever undid one slide) could be seen and not used. Fixed: the arrow is no longer drawn on the couch and the pause menu offers **Start this pond again** (slides so far still count).
- **Checked so far:** everything above has unit tests and recorded browser checks in headless Chrome. Nothing has been judged on an iPad, with real controllers on a TV, or with a child. What is deployed is whatever `origin/main` holds (`git log origin/main`).

### Next up, before it was rewritten as chunks

The open items of the old list as they were written, with their detail. The [roadmap](../ROADMAP.md#next-up) now gives each one a status, a next slice and a check tier.

Most sessions should build from this list; it never waits for the one after it. Check effort follows the [verification budget](../../AGENTS.md#verification-budget). Roughly in order:

- [ ] **Profiles first (decided 2026-10-09).** The landing screen becomes profiles; a person's age sets where the map and levels start; the Couch play button goes once each of its features has a home. Staged in [Profiles first](../ROADMAP.md#profiles-first-one-map-for-every-age): profiles and the chooser, then the island shell taking a controller, then a grown-up band and Lantern Lake, touch for grown-up games, and Game night on profiles. Stage 1's definition of ready is not yet written.
- [ ] **Grown-up play for one.** The couch route was built for two people taking turns; the courses are what held up when one person played. Make it something one grown-up (first of all, the developer's wife, mostly on the TV with a controller) picks up alone. The Settings page (step 1), a "Just me" start with a puzzle shelf and a best-route replay (step 2) and **Sudoku Garden** with its Six Beds and Three Big Beds courses (step 4) **Lantern Lights** with Dusk on the Pond (step 3a) and **Picture Logic** with Pond Pictures and The Big Pictures (step 5) and **Harbor Rush** (Ferry Jam on the couch, with Busy Harbors; step 3) **Word Search** with Pond Words and The Big Hunt (step 10), **Island Bridges** with Island Hopping (step 7) and **Pond Conga** with Crumb Trail (step 8, the first relaxing arcade game) are built; next are a chained-word game (Letter Pond), par courses on more of the built puzzles (step 3), another relaxing arcade game (Snowball Merge, Tile Pairs or Gem Swap), word games, and a keyboard-only typing extra. The plan, first slices and order are in [Grown-up play](../ROADMAP.md#grown-up-play-the-couch-route-for-one); the candidates are in [IDEAS](../IDEAS.md#grown-up-play-candidates-couch-route). The audience is parents and grandparents on tablets and laptops as well as controllers (AARP puts consoles at 28% of 50+ players): the [touch, laptop and grandparents notes](../IDEAS.md#touch-laptop-and-grandparents-2026-10-09) list what touch play would change in the written contracts, and a second batch of games.
- [ ] **Gap-review shortlist (proposal, 2026-10-09).** A review of bands, subjects and ladders against this roadmap found these holes: songs she already knows and filling and emptying for lap and toddler, writing numerals, time beyond the clock, reading a printed word, arrays and place value past 39, and no touch form of the solver-checked couch puzzles for about ages 8 and 9. Sketches, sourced research and the decisions it needs (a pentatonic exception for tunes, island forms of couch puzzles, in-game days only) are in [IDEAS](../IDEAS.md#gap-review-2026-10-09). Choose by an observed gap, state the definition of ready first, and prefer a mode of an existing game where the meaningful action is the same.
- [ ] **An older-child economy and progression pilot.** **Lemonade Stand is built (2026-10-08):** four levels from a weather forecast to a five-day market week with a purse, [below](../ROADMAP.md#first-slice-lemonade-stand-built-2026-10-08). Next, if it is fun, try one **Maker Harbor fair board** with earned tickets, child-chosen booth unlocks and transparent fortune wheels; if those choices are useful, add a voluntary Harbor Bank time deposit: the one proposed use of real elapsed time, because waiting and interest are the lesson. This is a new decision loop, not points pasted onto ordinary rounds; the staged definition and safeguards are [below](../ROADMAP.md#older-child-progression-a-market-week-not-an-island-grind).
- [ ] **Couch games that can score on seeded boards**, so face-off trips are not mostly team stops: Garden Rows (seeded beds suit a twin face-off; its grown-up form, Sudoku Garden, is built and plays seeded 6 by 6 beds in a trip), Treasure Map and Quick Tricks. Block Tower's fixed boards would only make a team stop; Owl Walk Home was considered and set aside. Each needs `control()`, `autoplay`, a catalog entry and a tier slot; `BROWSER_SUITE=couchgames COUCH_ONLY=<id>` proves it.
- [ ] **A second lap level for favorites:** 22 of the 37 lap games have one. A new lap level has to be appended at the end of a ladder, so first decide whether a band's range may skip levels. Peg Garden's bloom level also needs a guaranteed finish (repeated taps on one spot cannot complete it) before it opens further to lap play.
- [ ] **Give Wonder Woods a zone identity:** a host on the place, a small errand, an identity separate from the `school` band, and launch/return context carried through game navigation, preserving the existing age-trail routes. This is the first step of [E1](../ROADMAP.md#e1-early-elementary-roughly-ages-57).
- [ ] **A second story episode** with a distinct new consequence: a missing melody, a mixed-up delivery, or the Woodland Picnic using Mail Carrier's map. Use the [typed story result](../DESIGN.md#proposed-story-results) only when the episode carries something she made.
- [ ] **Diagnose the flaky Peg Garden touch suite:** `BROWSER_SUITE=batch BATCH_ONLY=pegs` fails on the development Mac at a different aimed level each run, on unmodified code too; a frame-timing difference between the simulation and the live game is a guess, not a finding.

### Next-up items that were finished

- [x] **Bouncy Launch: shown-pull predictions (2026-10-10).** School level 6 holds the pet at a fixed pull, lets the child tap the cloud they think it will reach, then releases the exact displayed pull. The same pure `pullFor`/`reach` mapping proves every shown pull lands on its cloud and drives the ghost finger, so a different prediction is an observation, not a miss. The level-relative how-to card and voice explicitly say that outcome. Rule tests and typecheck passed; the browser and finger-demo commands were attempted but the local runner ended their command sessions before either reached a final result, so neither is recorded as a pass.

- [x] **Pattern Train 10–12, “The seventh car” (2026-10-10).** The school ladder now asks for a seventh car from two complete ABC repeats, then links a changing color to a changing shape, then makes a numbered train count by 2, 5 or 10. The generator supplies the displayed rule, the three distinct choices and the shared ghost-finger/couch answer; the targeted level-12 play found and fixed a numeric hint glow that had assumed choice values were always 0–2. Typecheck, rule tests, a level-12 wrong-answer/hint/reward browser play and its clean ghost-finger demonstration passed. The couch trip’s fixed levels (4, 6 and 9) remain unchanged.

- [x] **Treehouse follow-ons (2026-10-09).** Taking a picture or tune down leaves the wall place empty and the back arrow restores it; every journal card names the exact action that reveals it; and the journal now adds Photo Safari's four successfully photographed action words, Seesaw Balance's two observed balance rules and Bouncy Launch's bigger-pull rule after two plainly different pulls. A game reports only what its completed round actually showed, so opening a game or seeing a rule only in its instructions finds nothing.
- [x] **No background music in games where she makes the music (2026-10-09; the developer asked for it).** Jelly Drums, Song Maker, Sound Garden, Rhythm Neighbors and Clap the Syllables now play a near-silent style (`silenced(STYLES.x)`, `src/audio/music.ts`) instead of the island's loop; Beat Builder and Pet Says already did. The style keeps playing at the game's own tempo, because Song Maker's playhead and Jelly Drums' bobbing read `music.beats()`, `beat()` and `bpm()` from it, which `music.stop()` would take away. The treehouse tune plaque dips the room's loop with `music.duck()` for as long as her kept song plays and lets it return on the audio clock. The how-to card, which opens before the round is built, switches to the quiet style as it opens, so the place's loop is not under a demonstration. Rhythm Neighbors is also a couch game and is quiet there too. A registry test names the seven music-making games and fails if one is given an audible style. Pattern Train's bell level is left as it was: the pattern, not the sound, is what she makes there. Listening on the iPad is [open below](../DEVICE-CHECKS.md#needs-a-person-or-a-device).
- [x] **Make real-game scene teardown safe for an already-ready continuation (2026-10-09).** An `await` continuation that was queued in the same frame its scene left still ran once and could set a property on a destroyed display object (Frog Hop's `leap`, found 2026-10-08 by opening and abandoning cards at random moments, about one in fifty, and again in Peekaboo Barn's `question()` loop reaching `Spot.go`). `Scene.destroy()` now stops scene-owned work immediately but releases the detached Pixi tree one task later, as `Demo` already did. A lifecycle test fails with synchronous destruction on the same null-transform write, and `GAME_ONLY=frog-hop,peekaboo-barn ROUNDS=20 node scripts/abandon-cards.mjs` opened 40 cards with no page errors. Game-owned timers and global callbacks still need their own guards. The production offline check now opens and reloads a rearranged treehouse, both kinds of kept creation and the journal.
- [x] A small **Discovery Marsh** investigation before any broader habitat simulation, with explicit observations and a limited, explainable model: Habitat Helpers (2026-10-09).

### Profiles stage 1: the definition of ready as written, and what was built

Written 2026-10-09 and built the same day in two slices (PRs #60 and #62). Slice 1a is the storage layer (`src/progress/profiles.ts` and the active-profile `Store`, described in [DESIGN.md](../DESIGN.md#persistence-and-grown-up-ui)). Slice 1b's checklist as written:

**Slice 1b: the chooser and what a person sees (next).** Ready when each of these is done:

1. The chooser replaces `StartScene` and `go.start()`. The first tap still unlocks audio and speech inside the gesture. The gear stays, and the Couch play button, the C key and the controller-opens-couch shortcut go (replaced by the mode choice above). `CouchScene`'s two "back to start" labels and the grown-ups' page's return route change with it.
2. Add-a-player: name, birth month and year (`checkBirthInput` for the clock rule), a friend (Pip in a chosen color until the pet `kind` exists), then the comfort questions. A pure `petSpecFor(pet)` so a card can draw another profile's friend (`petSpec()` reads the active profile).
3. The grown-ups' page splits into per-profile settings and device settings (sound, backup, reset). It gains a birth field and the *start here instead* choice (the band buttons become that once a birth is set). The age labels in `src/progress/bands.ts` ("Preschool 3–4 years", "Pre-K 5–6 years") change to match the boundaries; this is the one visible change a profile layer alone would cause, so it ships here. Deleting a person, resetting and restoring stay behind the gear's hold; backup uses `exportAll`.
4. Controller and keyboard navigation across the cards (`nextSpot` in `src/couch/focus.ts`, `ControllerSampler`; a controller press is not a gesture for audio, so the unlock still comes from a pointer or key).
5. After a switch: `applySettings()`, a scene rebuild, and clearing the session state keyed by band (`PlaceScene` `lastScroll` and `toldMore`, `SubjectPlaceScene` `positions`, `JournalScene` `lastPage`).
6. Checks: the `browser-check.mjs` preamble (every suite taps the title at 512,308), `phonefit`, `grownups` and every couch `enter()` helper, and `offline-check.mjs` (which also taps the title). Docs that name the title screen or the couch entry (README, COUCH-PLAY, DESIGN, AGENTS) change in the same PR.
7. A grown-up's voice set is not needed yet: with no stored type there is one set until stage 3 gives the `grownup` band its own.

**What was built, and what changed on the way:**

- Items 1 to 6 were built. The chooser is plain HTML over the island's backdrop (`ChooserScene`), the input that picks a card picks the mode, and Add a player fills the blank first profile. The grown-ups' page edits any player and returns to whoever was playing. The `phonefit`, `grownups`, couch and offline scripts moved off the title screen, and a new `profiles` suite covers the chooser.
- **The age labels** in `src/progress/bands.ts` changed to the accepted boundaries (under 2, 2, 3, 4–5, 6 and up), which also changed the small labels under each place on the map.
- **Added to the checklist while building:** a "Who's playing?" button top-left on the map (otherwise changing player needed a reload); the page returning to whoever was playing; a click with no pointer behind it (Enter, a screen reader) counting as a key, never as the island; keys and the controller stopped while the add form or the page is open; `selectPlayer` as the one place a player is switched.
- **Cut, and recorded in the roadmap:** the comfort questions, a device-level sound setting, a friend's `kind`, and linking couch records to people.

### Profiles stages 2 to 5, as written before the lands plan

On 2026-10-09 the developer chose themed lands for the map, and these four stages moved into the roadmap's [Lands](../ROADMAP.md#lands-navigation-and-discovery) stages B to E. As they stood:

2. **The island shell takes a controller.** Only `CouchScene` drives `control()` and the pause menu's *Start again* and *Show a hint* (`restart`, `askForHint`); `GameScene` does none of it. Before any grown-up game can live on the map, `GameScene` must sample keys and the controller, call `control()`, offer those two pause entries, and show the demonstration for the input in use. The games already read `ctx.couch?.course`, so a missing couch context is tolerated. If this proves large, Lantern Lake can instead hand a chosen game to the couch shell until it lands.
3. **The grown-up band and Lantern Lake.** Move the six couch-only games into the catalog and give the island couch games a grown-up range, with input gating. Price each moved game: a how-to card (couch how-tos live in `catalog.ts`), voice lines, registry subject and place coverage, a `smoke` case, and the README and roadmap counts.
4. **Touch and mouse for grown-up games**, a few at a time, with prompts that follow the last input.
5. **Game night on profiles.** Move trips, face-offs, courses, the shelf and records onto profiles, link the old couch records, retire unlock tiers and the couch shell, and archive the couch guide.

### Lands stage A: the definition as written, and what was built

Built 2026-10-09 (PR #74). The roadmap's [Lands](../ROADMAP.md#lands-navigation-and-discovery) section keeps the decision and the open stages; [DESIGN](../DESIGN.md#the-island-lands-and-a-home-spot) describes the result. The definition as written before the build:

**Fun action and purpose.** Choosing where to go on an island, meeting a host who suggests something, and finding games that are new. The learning purpose is the games'; this stage makes them findable, with every game for her age reachable in two taps from the map and nothing behind a gate.

- **Rules, pure and tested (`src/content/lands.ts`).** `LAND_IDS`; each land's name, spoken line (the subject's), host, backdrop, tune, map drawing and an explicit list of its games (at first the games of its subject, in registry order). `visibleIn(land, band)` and `olderIn(land, band)` split a land exactly; `visibleGames(band)` is what the home spot lists; `playBand` (moved from the picnic to `src/progress/bands.ts`); `suggest`; and the layout of the map and of a land's spots. Tests: every game in exactly one land; every land with a line, a host and at least one game at lap; at `school` nothing is behind a signpost; `playBand` always a band the game has; a land never outgrows one screen at any band, in landscape, portrait and a wide phone view, with every target at least 100 units and none overlapping (past capacity a land would page, never shrink).
- **Scenes.** `MapScene` becomes the island of lands plus the home spot and the picnic (players, gear, treehouse and book buttons stay); a land twinkles while it holds a new game; the pet waits at the last land; a birthday throws confetti and changes the home spot. `LandScene` is new: the land's backdrop, host, games and signpost on one screen, the map button top-left, the book top-right, the pet bottom-left. `PlaceScene` (and `SubjectPlaceScene`) become the home spot, listing every game for her age and younger.
- **Routes.** `go.land(id)`; `go.game(id, band?, story?, again?, origin?)` resolves the band through `playBand` and carries where the game was opened from; the chooser opens the map; the journal's arrow comes back to the journal.
- **Not in this stage:** the couch puzzles, a controller on the island, favorites on the map, a host who learns her name, and any change to the save (session memory only).
- **Checks:** the navigation tier: typecheck, unit tests, build, the rewritten `world` suite and a new `lands` case, `island`, `profiles`, `journal` and `phonefit` for the assertions that move, and the offline check.

**What was built, and where it differs.**

- **Rules** in `src/content/lands.ts` (`LAND_IDS`, `LANDS`, `visibleIn`, `olderIn`, `visibleGames`, `suggest`, `landLayout`, `mapLayout`), with `playBand` and `lowestBand` in `src/progress/bands.ts`. `playBand` implements the stated rule (own band, else the highest below, else the lowest above) rather than reusing the picnic's nearest-band sort, which agrees only while every game's bands are one unbroken run; the picnic's `bandForGame` is now `playBand`. `lands.test.ts` (13 tests) runs every land at every band in four views (`computeView` of an iPad either way up, a phone sideways, and the phone with a notch): an upright iPad is 1024 by 1365 logical units, not 768 by 1024, so there is more room upright than the first sketch assumed.
- **Layout.** A land's drawings shrink from 170×155 to at most 140×130 when a land is crowded, with the touch area (`spotRect`) always over 100 units; rows sit together a little above the middle of the ground. The fullest land today (Cozy Village, Puzzle Peaks and Tinker Lab at school, eleven games and the host) takes the 160×146 size in landscape.
- **Art** in `src/art/lands.ts`: ten map drawings, the hosts (Hazel moved there from Mail Carrier's woods, which now imports her), and the `Signpost`. The map draws each land at 0.85 scale with its name; the pet on the map is a decoration (`eventMode` none) beside the last land, so it never takes a tap meant for a spot.
- **Scenes.** `LandScene` builds both sets of games when it starts and the signpost only swaps which is visible. `MapScene` lost the trail; the birthday swaps the home spot's drawing with a burst. `PlaceScene` uses the shared `makeLandmark` (`src/ui/landmark.ts`) and lists `visibleGames`; its shelf and the subject cards' Favorites show every hearted game, so a bigger kids' game hearted after a signpost round is not lost. The places' map coordinates were removed with the trail.
- **Routes.** `Origin` in `src/app/App.ts`; `go.game` resolves `playBand` itself and keeps refusing an id that is not an island game (the couch-only games). The grown-ups' page lists levels at each game's play band and names a signpost game played this week as "a game for bigger kids".
- **Not built:** the couch puzzles (stage B), a controller on the island (stage C), favorites on the map, a host who learns her name (both now proposals in IDEAS), any change to the save.

### Habitat Helpers, the new-games item (built 2026-10-09)

- [ ] **New games from the shortlist:** **Habitat Helpers is built (2026-10-09)** for the science gap: Bunny and Duck each need food, water and shelter; the six-level ladder moves from obvious pieces to distractors, a prediction, compact multipurpose habitats and one three-piece garden compatible with both. Its definition of ready was two stylized visitors, explicit needs, prediction before a test, a compatible-needs puzzle, free experimental revisions, supported completion, discoveries only for visitors actually shown, the complete band ladder and normal game integration. Story Theater or Shape Buddies remain ([candidates](../IDEAS.md#candidates)).

### A bot demonstration on every game's how-to card: the build log

The item as it stood on 2026-10-09 with its per-bot rules, timings and fixes. Finished 2026-10-09: all 74 island games have one.

- [x] **A bot demonstration on every game's how-to card (started 2026-10-08, finished 2026-10-09).** The rule (the developer, 2026-10-08): **show the input the person is actually using.** On the island's card that is a ghost finger on a touch screen and a mouse pointer clicking and dragging on a desktop (the last real pointer decides, so a touch laptop follows whichever was used last); couch play keeps its controller and, on a laptop, key-cap demos, chosen by its own **Where I'm playing** setting. Before this item the island's card showed a bot only for the fifteen games the couch also plays, and that bot pressed controller buttons, which is wrong for touch; the rest were text alone. A game gains an optional `autotouch()` beside `autoplay`: it names what a capable child would do next (tap a spot on an object, drag an object to a spot, trace a path), and `Demo` plays it with a code-drawn hand that travels, presses and sends the events straight to that object's own `onTap`/`draggable` handlers (`emit` on the target, so the card's real Play and Back buttons are never reached and nothing is hit-tested). The game only chooses targets, by rules in `logic.ts` that a test can check; timing, the hand and the card's note belong to the harness. The demonstration plays **her current level** on a fixed seed, so it matches the card's "this level" line; couch demos keep the couch's own demo level. Where a game has `autotouch` the card shows the finger; a game without one is text alone. **The fifteen couch games come first**: they are the only island cards that show a controller highlight to someone holding a tablet or using a mouse, which is the thing this item exists to fix (all fifteen are done as of 2026-10-09: ten as plain taps and drags, and Egg Catch, Bounce Back, Bouncy Launch, Peg Garden and Bumper Garden as the first steering, pull-and-release and timed-tap bots). Then, in order of mechanic: the other taps, the other drags to a target, tracing and steering, the remaining window-listener games (Splish Splash, Rainbow Fingers) and free-play toys (a short flourish that need not finish). The couch's own demo stays a controller or key-cap demo, chosen by its Settings (the keyboard diagram lights the arrow keys, Enter, Backspace and Esc on a laptop). Each built game gets a check that its bot finishes at its first and last level. *Built so far (2026-10-09):* **45 of 74**: Bubble Pop (taps, all four modes), Shape Sorter (drags), Duckling Parade (steering with a dragged finger), and ten of the fifteen couch games: Memory Match, Frog Hop, Pattern Train, Light Lab, Penguin Slide, Secret Code, Robot Path, Rhythm Neighbors (a rhythm played by resting longer for a long gap), Sink or Float and Ferry Jam, and, as the first of the other taps (2026-10-09), eleven more: Jelly Drums (free play climbs and descends the scale; a tune round waits for the tune, then copies it), Song Maker (lights a song, or copies it from shadows, a card, a pattern or a tune heard), Beat Builder (boom-clap in free play; builds the faint, heard or half-given beat and presses the check where one is asked for), Rhyme Time, Opposites, Pet Says (the grown-up's arrow, once the pet has finished its move), Goodnight Room, Peekaboo Barn, Weather Wardrobe, Duck Pond and Photo Safari. Each bot's choice of target is a tested rule in `logic.ts` (several shared with the couch bot, which now calls the same rule; Pet Says is the exception, whose bot only presses the arrow once the pet has finished), plays its round with no wrong move and no hint at every level, and finishes inside 75 seconds (`BROWSER_SUITE=fingerdemo`; `FINGER_LEVELS` plays the middle levels too, and the longest plays are Duck Pond's last level at 66 s and Jelly Drums' last at 61 s). The harness, the card's note, the lowered effects (40% while a demonstration plays), the drag-lock fix and the `pause`/`receiver`/`when` fields are done. The last five couch games followed (2026-10-09): **Egg Catch** (a hen tapped; the basket slid under each brown egg by a tap beneath it and kept out of the way of white ones, never moving through a white egg's lane; the gates each egg still has to pass flipped; the bin the set gates lead to tapped), **Bounce Back** (the paddle placed where the ball will arrive, and on the stars level slid along so the bounce goes through the star), **Bouncy Launch** (the pet pulled back by the pull that lands on the cloud, or a clear step nearer or farther than last time; the pet now also hears `pointerup` itself, so the ghost's release reaches it, and a real release reaches both listeners harmlessly), **Peg Garden** (a pearl dropped over the buds still waiting, and the launcher swung to the angle whose shot touches the numbered flower; the aim search is now shared with the couch bot) and **Bumper Garden** (a tap to launch, and flips placed by a look ahead through the game's own flipper swing and kick). The bots for Peg Garden's drift and Bumper Garden's kick look at the game's next random draw through `Rng.clone()`, because a demonstration shows right play and a child cannot see a draw coming; Bumper Garden moves its ladybug and flippers in fixed 1/120-second steps (a game change that only makes it frame-rate independent), and its hand hovers over a flipper's button until the planned step, which the game holds for the one frame the press takes. All five finish clean at every level in 6 to 49 seconds (Bumper Garden's first level is the longest at about 49 s; its fifth varies from 12 to 45 s from run to run). Five more of the one-touch taps followed (2026-10-09): **Treasure Map** (the middle of the square asked for; the map's cell geometry now lives in `logic.ts`, so the finger and a real tap read one grid), **Feelings Faces** (free play shows each feeling in turn and then finishes; every question level taps the face, helper or friend that answers it), **Critter Crossing** (a visible gate: only critters that fit; a secret rule: the critter whose answer rules out the most pictures, the light bulb once the guess button has woken and one picture is left, then that picture), **Scoop Shop** (a new flavor on each scoop in free play, otherwise the scoop the order still needs; on the last level it waits for the picture to hide and never peeks) and **Picture Graph** (a block above each short bar, the check, then each question's column or number; one block for each pair on its keyed level 5, no build on level 6). All five play clean at every level in 7 to 31 seconds. Five more of the one-touch taps followed (2026-10-09): **Little Helpers** (taps the fruit to send a helper until the team is exactly big enough, then the whistle where there is one; never taps a helper, which would send it back), **Market Stall** (the fewest coins that make the price, one tap each, then the bell; on 'pay it two ways' a second, different set of coins), **Garden Rows** (the tightest spot first, with the seed packet it takes, so the packets are changed as little as possible), **Garden Grow** (a tap on each bed; the packet asked for, as many times as the sign shows; rain only when the seeds are exactly right), **Owl Walk Home** (flip a card, then hop the owl that card carries the farthest, which is also the pet's rule). Five more followed (2026-10-09): **Ramp Race** (the ramp tapped to the height a star needs, the floor tapped only where it can change, then go; the explore level changes the height before every roll so a roll beside the last shows that higher goes farther; a fair test changes only the one thing the question names), **Pixel Pictures** (copy levels tap the picture's squares and choose each color before its squares; the mirror level taps the right half only; the logic levels tap the squares the row and column numbers decide, nearest first, carrying the deduced empty squares itself since the game crosses one out only after a wrong tap), **Animal Snack** (free play visits each animal in turn, then taps the next snack, taps the animal asked about, carries each snack to its eater, and counts out exactly the number asked for before the bell), **Monster Munch** (all eight levels: taps, a drag to the monster, one cookie each, a fair share to whoever has the fewest, the bell on the right order, then the right number on the pad, and on the leftover level how many each and how many are left) and **Size Parade** (taps the biggest or smallest friend; carries each friend in size order to its place in the line). All play clean at every level in 6 to 56 seconds (the longest are Pixel Pictures' level 5 at 56 s and level 2 at 55 s). **Habitat Helpers**, the 74th game, followed in its review (2026-10-09): a building round places the fewest pieces that give the visitor, or both, food, water and shelter, then taps the gate; a prediction round taps the visitor the prepared garden really welcomes, then the gate; all of it a tested rule in `logic.ts`, clean at levels 1 to 6 in 9 to 18 seconds. **29 remain** (earlier counts said 72 and left Lemonade Stand out). Five of the sorting and choosing drags followed (2026-10-09): **Color Garden** (the next fruit carried to the basket of its color, let go over the middle of the mouth where six crowded baskets still each catch it), **Critter Sort** (each critter that fits a rule carried to the middle of its part of the diagram, a critter that fits neither left on the grass, and on the guess level a tap on the picture of the true rule; the hoop geometry now lives in `logic.ts`), **Story Steps** (the picture that comes next, to the first empty slot, never one from the other story), **Word Monsters** (each monster tapped in turn in free play, the answer tapped when finding by name, sound or first sound, the next letter dropped on the first empty slot, and a word family's first sound only) and **Clap the Syllables** (a clap for each beat with no rest between claps, because the game counts a word after 1.5 seconds of quiet and the hand's own trip and press leave about 0.9; each picture carried to the hoop for its beats; the picture with the played beats tapped). All play clean at every level in 5 to 44 seconds (the longest are Color Garden's last level at 44 s and Word Monsters' spell and family levels at about 33 s). Five of the placing and building drags followed (2026-10-09, later): **Bug Builder** (the color the next empty spot needs, carried from the tray to it in the order the bug is built, whether a faint ghost, a model bug or the mirrored wing shows it; the 57-unit slot rule moved into `logic.ts`), **Tangram Town** (the piece in hand turned with the turn button until it fits, taking the open place that needs the fewest turns, then carried there; never the help hand, which counts as a hint), **Puzzle Pals** (the first piece in the tray carried to the middle of its own place on the scaled frame), **Pet Kitchen** (the cut with the fewest pieces that share out evenly, so quarters only for one sandwich and four friends and never halves served short; each piece to the plate with the fewest; then serve; on the recipe levels fruit added until the recipe is doubled, then serve; the nearest-plate rule moved into `logic.ts`) and **Block Tower** (all six levels: a tap anywhere stacks and the next one knocks the tower down; the basket tapped up to the flag or Bear's tower, then the bell; a tap on the one tower whose blocks each have the middle over the block below; blocks carried one at a time to the places `finish()` finds for the star). All play clean at every level in 3 to 33 seconds (the longest are Puzzle Pals' twelve pieces at 33 s and Block Tower's basket levels at 26 to 29 s). **19 remain.** Then more of the other taps (the other one-touch games, a bot covers every level of its game, so Mail Carrier's school-band Wonder Woods map levels, which are a different class, and Stop and Go's self-running light, which the finger has to catch while it is green, are each worked out before they get one), drags, tracing (Letter Trails), the other window-listener games and free-play toys. The count lives here and in the README. *Open:* whether a hand where a finger goes reads clearly (the iPad check below), and whether 40% is the right level for the demonstration's sound.
  **Catalog count note (2026-10-09):** Habitat Helpers is the 74th game; it left its demonstration to the bot pass and received it in its review, so the count after the batch of five and Habitat Helpers is 45 of 74 with 29 remaining.

The five **drags with a consequence** followed (2026-10-09): **Seesaw Balance** (larger friends to the far tray; exact block or weight combinations; a three-present comparison tournament; matching removals from both sides before the box’s number), **Inchworm Measure** (worms laid end to end on each row before the exact reading), **Teddy Doctor** (the next boo-boo, or the right reusable tool at its body part and in check-up order), **Peekaround Island** (a turn before the hidden friend, the hidden friend’s card, or a named friend carried to a direction that fits), and **Chain Reaction** (loose ramps carried into the nearest complete working design, then Run). All five play clean at their first and last levels in 5 to 63 seconds; pure rules play the middle levels. **14 remain.** Then come tracing and steering, window-listener games and free-play toys, and the three one-touch games whose complete target rules still need working out. The count lives here and in the README. *Open:* whether a hand where a finger goes reads clearly (the iPad check below), and whether 40% is the right level for the demonstration's sound.

Five tracing and steering games followed (2026-10-09): **Letter Trails** (a sampled stroke reduced to its real turns, following every straight and curve through the game's zero-lift drag), **Dot Link** (a legal pair, requested-color chain, long chain or closed square), **Roundup** (a moving finger kept behind the next needed animal and aimed at its gate, then the exact-count bell), **Lasso Loops** (a searched rectangle containing exactly the fireflies the next jar asks for, followed by the right total) and **Clock Tower** (the needed real hand turned to its mark, the matching daily event or the bell). Their target and path rules are tested in `logic.ts` or the existing stroke rules; all five finish clean at their first and last levels in 5.5 to 60.4 seconds. This brought the live count to **65 of 74, with 9 remaining**.

Five games from the one-touch taps and the free-play toys followed (2026-10-09): **Stop and Go** (the toy light; at the send and walk levels the hand waits over the front car or the step button until the light is green and presses then, since `onTap` answers on touch-down; at the crossing `crossTouch` turns a green road with nobody left waiting red before the other road goes, and never leaves both green), **Mail Carrier** (the addressed house on the street levels; on the Wonder Woods map the neighbor's house, then on the route level each stop in letter order and the walk button), **Lemonade Stand** (`forecastChoice`: the best batch and price for the sign alone, taking the middle of the day's unseen 0 to 2 extra friends, so it cannot read the future; it never touches the lightbulb), **Stamp Studio** (`stampDemo`: five stamps with the level's stamps and colors, one turned and one made big where those buttons exist, one carried where stamps move, then the green arrow) and **Splish Splash** (`scrubPath`: a zig-zag whose brush leaves no mud behind, over the next splotch the question allows).

**Sound Garden** and **Rainbow Fingers** followed (2026-10-09): Sound Garden's hand taps each singer in turn in free play, the tile that shows the answer in the listening levels, and the woodpecker's rhythm on the drum, whose short and long gaps come from the hand's rest between taps (`echoRest`: the hand cannot tap faster than its own tap-and-return, so every echo is slowed together and a long gap is 1.7 short ones); Rainbow Fingers' hand makes five wavy strokes and then the frame in the free levels, a different color for each stroke in the pots level, and in the picture levels picks the pot (or pours the two primaries) for the picture's color and fills it with a zig-zag (`fillPath`, tested against `Coverage` at every size a page can draw a picture) before the frame.

The last two followed (2026-10-09): **Quick Tricks** (both classes: the first show's leaf that covers every friend carried over their middle, the partner sock to the monster, a stretched handle or the long enough plank across the river, `leafDrop`/`partnerSock`/`plankToPlace`; the second show's parcel turned until it fits and carried to its slot, a bowl to each empty place with the last bowl winning by itself, exactly the berries asked for and then submit, `encoreMove`, with the green arrow after each step) and **Fluffy Salon** (strokes through the fur with each tool in turn in free play and the mirror, and with a request the tool for what still needs doing and strokes until it is done, the mirror only once nothing is left; `salonMove` and `strokePath`, tested against the fur's own tool effects, which moved out of the game into `logic.ts` as `touchStrands`, `anchors` and `strandPoints`, unchanged).

**What the whole pass taught, for the next game that wants a bot:**

- Confirm the real input before planning around a scan: Splish Splash, Stamp Studio, Rainbow Fingers and Sound Garden had been listed as window-listener games, but their touches arrive on their own objects, so the ghost reached them with a `tap` or a `trace` and no change to the game.
- `onTap` answers on pointerdown, so the hand's `when` and its press are one moment (a car only on green needs no slack). The intent's `pause` sets the gap before the next touch, and the hand cannot tap faster than `MIN_TAP_GAP` (0.87 s); a game that judges a rhythm only by long against short gaps (Sound Garden's echo) is still demonstrated, by slowing every gap together (`echoRest`, tested against the game's own `judgeEcho`).
- A toy's bot must end the round the way a person does, because `fingerdemo` asks for a finished round with no wrong move (Stamp Studio: five stamps and the green arrow; Fluffy Salon: a stroke with each tool, then the mirror), and a bot must choose from what the player can see, never hidden state (Lemonade Stand's `forecastChoice`).
- A game that applies an effect once per pointer event (Fluffy Salon's fur) depends on the frame rate: the app caps a step at 0.05 s, so the hand's events can be 21 units apart, and the test runs the bot's strokes at that spacing and at 7, with an estimate of the hand's time against the level's 15-second hint timer.
- A demonstration window narrows the card's text column, so the card gets taller: measure every level's card in landscape and portrait, not only the default level the `howto` suite checks, and trim the copy before touching the layout (Stamp Studio's and Lemonade Stand's had to be trimmed).
- A bot that only plays right can still be told off by an overlap: Splish Splash counted a miss when a stroke slid from a just-cleaned ear into the head's patch; a finger still within reach of a patch it scrubbed in the same touch is no longer told off, and one that slides on to a different part still is (`wrongTouches`). Probe an unclean demonstration (`misses > 0`) before changing a rule, and compare coordinates in one space.

### Short ladders: what was built and audited

#### Word Monsters 8: Change the end (2026-10-10)

The school ladder now reaches level 8. Keep the first two sounds in place and change the last sound to make the pictured word: cat, cap or can. The two spare endings make no other word with that beginning. New code-drawn cap and can pictures join the existing cat; three rounds use the same drag-to-slot interaction as level 7. The level has its own spoken instruction and scoped how-to step, and the bot chooses only the final letter. Device speech remains a stand-in for phonics and needs an iPad check.

#### Robot Path 11: Where will it stop? (2026-10-10)

The school ladder now asks the player to read a shown program, choose the square where the robot will stop, then watch it play. Both individual arrows and counted arrows are shown, with a rock that makes the actual stopping square differ from skipping the rock or reading the arrows backwards. Predictions are free experiments; a wrong guess is never a miss. The cell targets are 100 logical units across. The level has its own spoken instruction and scoped how-to step, and the ghost finger predicts and plays the program. Rule tests, the filtered level-11 round and its ghost-finger demonstration passed. iPad target review and device speech remain open.

#### Robot Path 12: Fix the program (2026-10-10)

The school ladder now reaches level 12. Three authored counted programs stop one step short after a free first watch. The player selects the final slot and taps its arrow once to add the missing step; each board has exactly one single-slot edit that reaches the star. The hint glows on that slot after two failed repairs, and the ghost finger watches, selects, edits and plays. Typecheck, seven Robot Path logic tests, the filtered level-12 browser play and the level-12 ghost-finger demo passed. A portrait screenshot was captured; device target sizing, speech and child play remain open.

#### Treasure Map 5: two landmark clues (2026-10-10)

Treasure Map's school range now reaches level 5 without changing its first four saved levels or pins. Each four-find round puts two different visible landmarks on the five-by-five parchment: a horizontal relation fixes the target column and a vertical relation fixes its row. The spoken, pictured clue card presents them in order; two wrong digs leave the usual marks and turn on the two actual landmark-to-target lines. Generated targets, landmarks and offsets stay in bounds and distinct, and the ghost finger continues to dig the generated target. Typecheck, the Treasure Map rule tests, a level-5 real-tap browser round (two wrong digs, hint, reward and portrait review), and its clean level-5 finger demonstration passed.

#### Critter Sort 5: a not hoop (2026-10-10)

Critter Sort's school range now reaches level 5 without changing its first four saved levels or pins. Its picture sign has a red slash: the hoop is for critters that are **not** like the picture, while the critters outside share the pictured property. The usual two-miss glow directs attention to a critter that belongs in the hoop, and the spoken correction names whether that critter belongs in or out. The rule tests check the complement, generated rounds and the ghost-finger choice; typecheck, the targeted rule tests, the level-5 real-tap browser round (two wrong drops, hint, reward and portrait review), and its clean level-5 finger demonstration passed.

#### Duck Pond 11: hiding ducks (2026-10-10)

Duck Pond's school range now reaches level 11. The sign gives a whole from 5 to 10; some ducks paddle beneath a code-drawn bridge, and the child finds the hidden part on lily pads. A wrong pad counts on aloud from the visible ducks to the whole. Seeded rule tests check each part and the answer choices; typecheck, the Duck Pond rule tests, a level-11 browser round with two wrong answers, a hint, completion and reward, and the clean level-11 ghost-finger demonstration passed. The how-to card, voice and game guide describe the new level; no build was needed because shared files and assets did not change. Device speech, iPad target sizes and child play remain open.

- [ ] **Deepen short ladders** where a meaningful next decision exists: 25 games top out at four or five levels. Append levels without renumbering saved levels or pins. *Built (2026-10-09):* **Inchworm Measure 5 and 6** (use the ruler for how much longer one of two things is when they start at different marks, then how long two things are end to end; the gap between the ends and the mark where the first one stops are the offered wrong answers) and **Picture Graph 5 and 6** (a key where each block is two critters: build the bars from pairs, then read a keyed graph; the number of blocks is the offered wrong answer), both school-band top levels, both rule-tested, with Picture Graph's ghost finger covering them. *Next candidates, chosen by a new decision rather than a bigger number:* **Ramp Race** (predict where the car stops, then roll; its finish rule and the half-marks the model produces, such as 1.5 × 3, are worked out before it is built; a recorded-trials table is a new screen and belongs with the middle-elementary row), **Treasure Map** (a position built from two clues), **Critter Sort** (a "not" hoop) and **Penguin Slide** (a moss path); the older-band ceilings of Opposites, Goodnight Room, Stop and Go and Clap the Syllables should wait for a reason to grow them. *Audited (2026-10-09):* every school-reaching ladder was read in the [short-ladder audit](IDEAS-SHORT-LADDER-AUDITS-2026-10-09.md#short-ladder-audit-2026-10-09). 25 games have a whole ladder of four or five levels (12 reach school) and 23 give the school band only one or two levels, 33 school-reaching games in all. Seven have a small, real next decision, in order of cost and breadth: **Pet Kitchen 7–8** (name a fraction, halve a recipe; no new art), **Memory Match 10–12** (the same idea as a number word, a fraction name or a clock; the word cards are text, the fraction and clock cards need small new art and a legibility check), **Pattern Train 10–12** (the seventh car, two things changing together, counting by 2, 5 and 10; numbered cars are a new car kind), then Treasure Map, Critter Sort, Bouncy Launch and Rainbow Fingers. Fourteen more need a new interaction, art, a stated model or a decision first (Block Tower's top level is already its optimization puzzle), and twelve should stay as they are (a toy, a memory step, or already at a natural end). Proposals, with sketches for the first three.

### Grown-up play: the built steps

Steps 1, 2, 3a, 3b, 4, 5, 7, 8 and 10 of the couch route's plan, as written. Steps 3, 6, 8b, 9, 11 and 12, which are still open, stay in the [roadmap](../ROADMAP.md#grown-up-play-the-couch-route-for-one); step 10's open part (Letter Pond) is there too.

1. **Couch settings (built 2026-10-07).** A **Settings** button on the couch start page, reachable with a controller alone, holds **Where I'm playing** (*Choose for me*, the default, picks *TV with a controller* when a controller is connected or the window is TV-sized and *Laptop with keyboard* otherwise, and says why; either can be fixed), which swaps the controller picture, button glyphs and menu hints for a drawn keyboard, key caps and key names; **Sound** (a couch volume in five steps, and a music switch); **Text size** (normal, large, extra large, on top of the window scale); **Names** (moved from Controller setup); and **Couch backup** (moved here). The pause menu has the sound and text buttons too. A bounded `settings` object in the couch save has defaults and repair; it was added without a version step, as names and courses were, so a build from before it reads the same save and an old save opens with the defaults (a bump would have let a cached older build discard her progress). Rules and tests: `src/couch/settings.ts`, `BROWSER_SUITE=couchsettings`. *Not built:* **Tablet by touch** and a stage that takes pointer input need a game that supports touch on the couch; a filter that offers a keyboard-only game only on Laptop waits for such a game (step 11). The dead-arrow bug is still solved per game (hide the control when `ctx.couch` is set), not by this page.
2. **"Just me" and a puzzle shelf (built 2026-10-07).** *Fun action:* pick a puzzle and beat your own best. *Skill:* planning and deduction, in whatever game she picks. *Built:* a **Who is playing?** choice (*Just me* or *Two players*) on the couch start page and Settings, kept as `settings.players` (default *Two*, so an older save plays as before). *Just me* hides Player 2 everywhere (the start page, the course page's second card, the resume button's name, turn cards, the second name field), offers a Together-only **Start a trip** (a face-off needs two), and turns Challenges into the **Puzzle shelf**: every puzzle open, none to unlock, each showing her best and how far it is above par, such as "21 slides · 3 above par (18)". The three courses are the first entries. **Watch the best routes** on a Penguin Slide course page replays the solver's slides on the real pond, one pond at a time, scored and saved nowhere; it is offered after a run finishes or once the course has been finished, never in the middle of a run (it would be a hint she did not ask for). Records stay Player 1's, so switching back to two loses nothing. *Support:* unchanged (a hint marks the run helped). *Deeper play:* new shelf entries as they land; each course is automatically a shelf entry. *Not built:* a replay for Cloud Hopper (its best way is a landing preview, not a route); a shelf that mixes in one-off rounds of the other couch games (a shelf entry today is a course). Rules and tests: `src/couch/shelf.ts`, `src/couch/settings.ts`, `BROWSER_SUITE=couchsolo`.
3b. **Harbor Rush: Ferry Jam on the couch (built 2026-10-08).** *Fun action:* slide boats along their lanes so the red ferry can sail out to the dock (Rush Hour). *Skill:* planning a sequence of moves. *Built:* Ferry Jam, an island game, gained a couch mode in the style of Penguin Slide, with its 5 by 5 and 6 by 6 levels (3 to 6) played by grown-ups: the stick moves a highlight to the nearest boat in the direction pushed (`boatToward`, tested to reach every boat of all 84 harbors, always, even as the boats move), the bottom button picks the boat up and sets it down, the stick slides a held boat along its lane one cell at a time (with hold-to-repeat), the left button puts a held boat back for nothing or takes the last slide back (still counted), and the pause menu has **Show a hint** (the solver's next slide, marking a run helped) and **Start this harbor again** (slides kept). A slide is a boat moved any distance and counts when it ends somewhere new. Touch play is unchanged; on the couch the round arrow is not drawn and the automatic hint after many slides is off (a hint is asked for). One puzzle-shelf course of frozen harbors from the game's own solver-checked list, **Busy Harbors** (five harbors climbing from 8 to 18 slides, par 64), which also **replays its best routes** like the Penguin Slide courses; the bot follows the solver, so it always plays a fewest-slides way. A trip climbs levels 3 to 6; a face-off compares slides over the fewest. *Not built:* a boat picked up with a diagonal stick push, a 7 by 7 size, two-cell-per-press sliding. Rules and tests: `src/games/ferry-jam/`, `BROWSER_SUITE=couchharbors`.
3a. **Lantern Lights (built 2026-10-07).** *Fun action:* press paper lanterns on a pond at dusk, each press flipping a lantern and the four beside it, until every lantern glows. *Skill:* planning a set of presses (pressing is its own undo and order never matters, so a win is a set of lanterns). *Built:* a second couch-only game (`GROWNUP_GAMES`) from the Lights Out family: boards from 3 by 3 to 5 by 5 made by pressing lanterns on an all-lit pond (so every board can be lit), with the fewest presses worked out **exactly** by Gaussian elimination over two numbers (tested against a brute force over every 3 by 3 and 4 by 4 board and over 4000 5 by 5 boards, including the ones that cannot be lit); a hint (pause menu **Show a hint**) points at a lantern in a fewest-press way and always brings the fewest down by exactly one (tested); the cursor shows what a press would flip; **Start this pond again** keeps the presses counted. *Score:* every press counts, a press taken back with the left button stays counted; par is the fewest. One puzzle-shelf course of frozen ponds, **Dusk on the Pond** (a 4 by 4 and four 5 by 5 ponds, par 36); trips play six levels and a face-off compares presses over the fewest. *Not built:* a 6 by 6 level (its one winning set is unique and often long), lantern colors or three-state lanterns, a Watch the best way replay (the bot's route is exactly the hint, but no replay screen). Rules and tests: `src/games/lantern-lights/`, `BROWSER_SUITE=couchlanterns`.
4. **Sudoku Garden (built 2026-10-07).** *Fun action:* place numbers by deduction. *Skill:* logical elimination. *Built:* a couch-only game (`GROWNUP_GAMES`, so the child's island never lists it) with 6 by 6 beds (2 by 3 boxes) and 9 by 9 beds drawn from the seed; a generator that keeps a bed only if it has exactly one solution and the plan's number of empty squares and grade, where the grade is the hardest technique a person needs (a lone number, an only place, a pair or a pointing line; tested sound against the solution); a number tray and pencil marks on a controller, with hold-to-repeat movement; a pause-menu **Show a hint** that names a wrong number first and otherwise the next number the easy steps can place, with the reason written and spoken and the pair marked; and **Start this bed again**. *Score:* every number placed is one entry (a replaced number costs another; taking one out and pencil marks are free); par is a bed's empty squares. Two puzzle-shelf courses of frozen beds: **Six Beds** (six 6 by 6 beds, par 124) and **Three Big Beds** (9 by 9, one of each grade, par 149); trips play the 6 by 6 levels and a face-off compares entries over the empty squares. *Not built:* a keyboard digit entry (the tray works with arrows and Enter), a daily or random bed on the shelf, a shared-cursor co-op form (step 12). Rules and tests: `src/games/sudoku-garden/`, `BROWSER_SUITE=couchbeds`, `BROWSER_SUITE=couchgames COUCH_ONLY=sudoku-garden`.
5. **Picture Logic (built 2026-10-08).** *Fun action:* fill a grid from the run numbers and a picture appears. *Skill:* line logic, one row or column at a time, with no guessing. *Built:* a third couch-only game (`GROWNUP_GAMES`) with a bank of ten 10 by 10 pictures (five gentler, five that need more passes) and three 15 by 15 pictures, each drawn as rows of characters in palette letters and checked to be solvable by line logic alone (tested), plus the 6 by 6 pictures Pixel Pictures can already set as logic puzzles; the finished picture takes its colors and says what it is. The bottom button fills (a filled square empties), the left button crosses (a crossed square lifts), and **holding** either while the stick moves paints a whole run (`PlayerControls.hold` and `holdUndo` are new in the controller sampler); a line's clue turns grey when its runs match and red when no way of filling it fits, from the clue alone, never from the answer. A pause-menu **Show a hint** names a mark that cannot be right first, then a square one line decides, with the line and its clue spoken and written; **Empty this picture** keeps the fills counted. *Score:* every fill counts (a filled square emptied and filled again counts twice; crosses and emptying are free); par is the picture's filled squares. Two puzzle-shelf courses of frozen pictures: **Pond Pictures** (a sailboat, a cat and a duck, 10 by 10, par 182) and **The Big Pictures** (a lighthouse and a butterfly, 15 by 15, par 230); trips play the 6 by 6 and 10 by 10 levels. *Not built:* a Watch the best way replay, a picture of her own choosing, colored (multi-clue) puzzles, a 20 by 20 size. Rules and tests: `src/games/picture-logic/`, `BROWSER_SUITE=couchpictures`.
7. **Island Bridges (built 2026-10-08).** *Fun action:* lay plank bridges between numbered islands (Hashi). *Skill:* counting and connection logic. *Built:* a fifth couch-only game (`GROWNUP_GAMES`): islands on a 7 by 7, 9 by 9 or 11 by 11 sea, each needing as many planks as its number (one or two on a bridge, nothing crossing, every island in one group). The generator grows a layout and its planks together and keeps a puzzle only if a propagation-and-search solver proves it has **exactly one solution** (checked in tests against a brute force over every plank assignment of thousands of small layouts, and a hand-made two-solution case); the two gentler levels are kept only if counting alone, with no guessing, finishes them. The stick moves the highlight to the nearest island in the direction pushed (`islandToward`, tested to reach every island), the bottom button arms an island to lay planks (a push toward a neighbor lays one, two at most, a bridge that would cross another or an island with no room bumps and costs nothing), the left button arms taking planks off (free), and an island turns green when it has its planks and its outline red when its number can no longer be reached (judged from the numbers alone). A pause-menu **Show a hint** names a plank that does not belong first, then an edge one island decides (its number equals the room it has left), then any plank the answer needs; **Clear this sea** keeps the planks counted. *Score:* every plank laid counts, taking one off is free; par is the planks in the answer. One puzzle-shelf course of frozen seas, **Island Hopping** (five seas, 8 to 22 islands, par 120); trips play the 7 by 7 and 9 by 9 levels. *Not built:* a Watch the best way replay, a 13 by 13 size, a technique-graded hint like Sudoku's. Rules and tests: `src/games/island-bridges/`, `BROWSER_SUITE=couchbridges`.
8. **Pond Conga (built 2026-10-08), the first relaxing arcade game.** *Fun action:* steer a line of ducklings to each crumb of bread in turn, round lily pads. *Skill:* planning a route and steering to it. *Built:* a sixth couch-only game (`GROWNUP_GAMES`): a line paddles on at a steady, gentle pace (2.4 to 3.4 steps a second, never faster as a pond goes on) and the stick turns the leader; nothing moves until the first turn. A bump into a lily pad, the bank or the line itself is a **bonk**: nothing is lost, the whole conga turns about (the old tail leads), there is a breather, and the bonk is one more step, so the line can never box itself in and there is no game over. *Score:* every step and every bonk counts, and the fewest is **exact**: the lily-pad-aware distances between the crumbs added up is a lower bound no route can beat, and a pond is used only if a route with no bonk really reaches it (`makePond` and `parsePond` prove it with the line's own body simulated). *This differs from the idea as first written* (fewest bonks, then time): a bonk count of zero is trivial to know and rewards nothing (circling slowly costs nothing), so the score is steps against a real best route, as in the slide and plank puzzles. Six levels (crumbs and lily pads grow before the pace does), a pause-menu **Show a hint** (dots along the way, which keep up as the line moves; marks a run as helped) and **Start this pond again**, the next crumb shown with a faint look at the one after it so a route can be planned, a bot that follows the pond's own route (so a bot run is exactly par), and one puzzle-shelf course of four frozen ponds, **Crumb Trail** (par 34 + 50 + 77 + 98 = 259), with a replay of the best routes. A trip plays one pond (levels 1 to 5); a face-off compares steps over the fewest. *Not built:* two lines on one pond, a wrapping pond, a spoken count. Rules and tests: `src/games/pond-conga/`, `BROWSER_SUITE=couchconga`.
10. **Word games on a controller.** **Word Search is built (2026-10-08).** *Fun action:* sweep a straight line across a grid of letters to find the words in a themed list. *Skill:* visual scanning, spelling, vocabulary. *Built:* a fourth couch-only game (`GROWNUP_GAMES`) from nine themed word lists kept as data in `words.ts` (pond, garden, kitchen, weather, farm, bakery, sea, woods, a cozy evening: plain common words of three to eight letters), grids of 8 by 8 (words across and down), 10 by 10 (slants too), 12 by 12 (every direction, words reading backwards) and 14 by 14, made so that every listed word occurs exactly once and no word on a short block list (`blocked.ts`: profanity and slurs) can be read in any direction by accident, and a theme word that hides one is a test failure. The bottom button marks a word's first letter and, once the cursor is on its last letter, checks the line (a crooked mark cannot be checked and costs nothing, the left button lets go of a mark, and a word reads either way); found words get a colored band and a struck-through entry in the list, and the word is spoken. A pause-menu **Show a hint** rings a word's first letter and names the word (marking a run helped), and **Search this grid again** keeps the guesses counted. *Score:* every checked line is a guess, right or wrong; par is the number of words. Two puzzle-shelf courses of frozen grids: **Pond Words** (pond, garden and weather; par 25) and **The Big Hunt** (two 14 by 14 grids, par 24); trips play the 8 by 8 to 12 by 12 levels. *Not built:* **Letter Pond** (a word chain walked with the d-pad), which needs a reviewed dictionary and not just themed lists; typed answers; her own word lists. Rules and tests: `src/games/word-search/`, `BROWSER_SUITE=couchwords`.

3. **Ice Blocks, as Penguin Slide level 6 (built 2026-10-10).** *Fun action:* slide into a block of ice and watch it skate; park it to make a stop where there is none. *Skill:* planning with a thing that moves. *Built:* `move()` in `src/games/penguin-slide/logic.ts` is a slide, or (standing beside a block that can move) a push that leaves the penguin where it is; a block skates until a rock, the edge, another block, a fish (never covered) or soft snow, and one that cannot move is a rock. The solver searches penguin, fish eaten and block positions (the optional `blocks` list defaults to none, so every older board and both couch courses are unchanged). Level 6 (school, one block, one fish, 3 to 5 slides) is generated so that it needs a push: with the blocks frozen like rocks the fish is out of reach or farther, tested over 150 seeds. Undo and restart put blocks back, the hint arrow is memoized per state, and `slide.ice` and one how-to line explain it. Couch trips and courses play levels 4 and 5 only, so level 6 never reaches the couch. Not built: a second block level, a block course, a device check of the block's look.

### Lemonade Stand: the plan as written before it was built

The plan that preceded the build. What was built, and where it differs, is in the [roadmap](../ROADMAP.md#first-slice-lemonade-stand-built-2026-10-08); the game's behavior is in [GAMES.md](../GAMES.md#lemonade-stand).

The plan as written before it was built:

**Why it is a new game.** Market Stall teaches composing a known amount with coins: the child pays, makes a second combination or gives change. Lemonade Stand asks her to choose uncertain quantities, observe a small system across days and revise a plan. It may reuse the stall, shell coins, critters and weather art, but putting it after Market Stall's level 6 would make one saved ladder hold two unrelated interactions. Give it its own stable ID and records; first support the `school` band, with later 8–11 modes belonging to Maker Harbor or another elementary zone rather than a new grade gate.

- **Fun action:** look at a lively weather-and-crowd forecast, choose how much lemonade to make, open the striped stand, and watch critters arrive until the SOLD and LEFT jars settle. The payoff is an immediate little crowd scene followed by one more informed choice.
- **Learning goal:** interpret a forecast and a picture table; compare made, sold, left and shells earned; reason about demand; change one variable after evidence. Deeper play adds unit cost, revenue and profit without pretending one simulated market is a law of real life.
- **Smallest complete round:** three days. Each day shows a spoken picture forecast and three large batch cards (for example 4, 8 or 12 cups). Demand is deterministic from the round seed and visible conditions. Opening the stand animates sales, then adds one row to a picture table. After day three, the pet points out one comparison and the round finishes with its ordinary sticker. Extra cups go to a picnic table for friends; they are not thrown away. The first modes cannot run out of shells.
- **Starting support:** the first plan has one changing factor, weather, and its three demand bands never overlap. The forecast and the previous table row remain replayable. A help request circles two comparable rows and says one useful relationship, such as “Both days were hot. More friends came when a cup cost two shells.” Choosing a weak batch or price is an experiment, not a miss; it produces honest feedback and the next day remains playable. Requested help counts as a hint, but sales never become adaptive misses.
- **Deeper modes:** add a visible event to the forecast (a ferry arriving or a quiet rainy morning); then choose a price of 1, 2 or 3 shells as well as quantity; then run a five-day market week with a fixed starter purse and simple ingredient cost. Price and weather each matter, customers give short picture/speech feedback, and the table can hold one variable still for a fair comparison. In this older mode a rainy overproduction day may produce a real operating loss: the leftover lemonade is shared, but its ingredients still cost shells. The loss is small, causal and recoverable; there is no debt, bankruptcy or failed week, and the host supplies a clearly named minimum restock if the purse cannot fund the next day's smallest batch. Recipe quality and advertising wait until those two choices are readable; the first build does not need Coolmath's full supply shop.
- **Stopping and rewards:** every planned day completes, with a clear “close the stand” option between days and a saved in-progress week only if the first play shows three days are too long for one sitting. The purse exists only inside this round. A result may save one bounded market-week chart to the journal later, but shells never buy stickers or determine whether the round completes. A low-profit week is evidence to interpret, not a miss.
- **Rules and checks:** put demand, price response, costs, forecasts, table rows and help selection in `logic.ts`. Over many seeds, assert demand stays within the prepared batch choices; hotter/equally priced comparable days never lower the modeled demand; a higher price never increases the same day's demand; revenue, cost and leftovers reconcile exactly; every help comparison is true and suggests a change that can affect the next day; and reloading the same seed reconstructs the same week. One filtered browser play should cover the first and deepest initial mode, a low-sales experiment, requested help, the end table and portrait.
