### The Fun and arcade review: why they play, and the evidence

The stance paragraph and the evidence labels of the 2026-10-09 [fun and arcade review](../IDEAS.md#fun-and-arcade-review-2026-10-09), moved here. The review keeps its two tracks, its Lander and Drift sketch, its decisions and its comfort-options audit.

**Why they play, and the stance this takes.** AARP's 50+ players give fun (86%), relaxation (79%), keeping minds sharp (78%), being challenged (74%) and stress relief (71%); 77% see play as part of healthy aging; 69% say games are not designed with them in mind, and ads are a major annoyance (the couch has none). Ease of play ranked highest. De Schutter and Vanden Abeele argue for *playfulness over usefulness* and *growth over decline*, which matches "fun, not educational": no skill is claimed, and a game is described as a game with a score. **No game here is offered as a health measure.** The brain-training evidence is narrow: a large review found training improves the trained tasks, with little transfer to distant tasks and everyday life (Simons et al., 2016). NeuroRacer (46 adults aged 60 to 85, 12 hours on a custom adaptive driving-and-sign game) cut a multitasking cost from 64% to 16%, stable six months later, but it was small and custom-built. The 2026 ACTIVE follow-up found about a 25% lower dementia diagnosis rate for one arm only, speed-of-processing training with booster sessions. None of this supports a game proposed here, and none should be cited as if it did.

**Evidence labels.** *Opened:* Wikipedia's Lunar Lander page, AARP's 2023 report, the Game Accessibility Guidelines list, the NeuroRacer paper on PubMed Central, the Association for Psychological Science's summary of Simons et al., and the University of Florida page on ACTIVE (which does not state limitations; coverage elsewhere notes selection bias among those who completed the training). *Search excerpts only, not opened:* De Schutter and Vanden Abeele; the reaction-time figures (simple reaction time slows by about 20 to 40 ms from 20 to 65, choice reaction time by about 90 to 120 ms, from a secondary review of Der and Deary); the Lunar Lander crash thresholds, fuel scoring and reviews of later versions (StrategyWiki, Arcade History, game sites); the puzzle sub-types among 50+ players (matching 48%, number placement 32%, hidden objects 25%). Sources: [Lunar Lander (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_Lander_(video_game)), [AARP 2023](https://www.aarp.org/research/topics/technology/info-2023/2023-gamers-50-plus.html), [Game Accessibility Guidelines](https://gameaccessibilityguidelines.com/full-list/), [NeuroRacer](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3983066/), [Simons et al.](https://www.psychologicalscience.org/publications/brain-training.html), [ACTIVE follow-up](https://ufhealth.org/news/2026/decades-later-brain-training-lowers-dementia-risk), [De Schutter and Vanden Abeele](https://lirias.kuleuven.be/bitstream/123456789/270075/3/De%20Schutter_VandenAbeele_2008_Meaningful%20Play%20in%20Elderly%20Life.pdf), [Der and Deary](https://www.research.ed.ac.uk/en/publications/age-and-sex-differences-in-reaction-time-in-adulthood-results-fro/), [Lunar Lander (StrategyWiki)](https://strategywiki.org/wiki/Lunar_Lander).

### The Touch, laptop and grandparents review: evidence labels

**Evidence labels for this round.** *Opened:* AARP's games portal (what it chose to feature), AARP's 2023 report (read earlier), and Wikipedia's peg solitaire page (about 23.5 million reachable positions on the English board, which exhaustive search covered in 1999; a shortest solution of 18 moves). *Search excerpts only, not opened:* the touch-target studies, the tremor and gesture studies, the intergenerational reviews, the FreeCell and Klondike figures, and the Copyright Office points (the game-specific wording is from an archived 2004 fact sheet; only the opening of Circular 33 was retrieved). Sources: [AARP games](https://www.aarp.org/games/), [AARP 2023](https://www.aarp.org/research/topics/technology/info-2023/2023-gamers-50-plus.html), [peg solitaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peg_solitaire), [W3C summary of target-size research](https://www.w3.org/WAI/GL/mobile-a11y-tf/wiki/Summary_of_Research_on_Touch/Pointer_Target_Size), [Jin, Plocher and Kiff](https://www.springerprofessional.de/en/touch-screen-user-interfaces-for-older-adults-button-size-and-sp/2824982), [Leitão and Silva](https://mural.maynoothuniversity.ie/6045/), [tremor and touch (RWTH Aachen)](https://hci.rwth-aachen.de/publications/wacharamanotham2011b.pdf), [touch gestures for older people](https://redi.cedia.edu.ec/document/238834), [intergenerational digital games review](https://research-portal.uu.nl/en/publications/bene%EF%AC%81ts-and-factors-in%EF%AC%82uencing-the-design-of-intergenerational-di/), [Open University on grandparent board games](https://www.open.ac.uk/blogs/news/arts-social-sciences/psychology/board-games-how-playing-them-online-can-bring-grandparents-and-grandchildren-closer-together), [FreeCell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FreeCell), [Klondike winnability](https://jair.org/index.php/jair/article/view/17167), [Circular 33](https://www.copyright.gov/circs/circ33.pdf), [Copyright Office game fact sheet (archived)](https://webharvest.gov/peth04/20041015023438/http://www.copyright.gov/fls/fl108.html).

### Cozy games, motivations and profiles: the evidence

The whole evidence section behind [Profiles first](../ROADMAP.md#profiles-first-one-map-for-every-age). The four "Use" conclusions are kept in the [idea notebook](../IDEAS.md#cozy-games-motivations-and-profiles-2026-10-09).

### Cozy games, motivations and profiles (2026-10-09)

The evidence behind [Profiles first](../ROADMAP.md#profiles-first-one-map-for-every-age) and the cozy rows above. **All of it is from search excerpts and none was opened**, so it is background and not proof.

- **What older players want less of.** Quantic Foundry's survey work, as covered by the press in 2016 (the sample size was reported inconsistently), found Competition falls most with age among its twelve motivations, Strategy holds steadiest, and no motivation grows with age. *Use:* the grown-up place leads with low-competition play, and a face-off is an option and never the default.
- **What "cozy" means.** There is no agreed definition. The most consistent marker in essays and blogs is the absence of a fail state, with low stakes, no timers, gentle art, and tending, making or decorating; one journal article surveyed 277 cozy-game players and notes that little is known about who they are. *Use:* the project's rules already match, so the cozy builders, Unpack the Room and Cottage Garden belong. A blog's market statistics were not used.
- **Tweens.** YPulse's 2026 report on 1,500 children aged 8 to 12 names Roblox, Minecraft and Fortnite as where friendships happen, with building platforms leading. *Use:* building, creating and obstacle-course games for a 9 to 12 place, not shooters (Fortnite is outside the boundaries).
- **Starting from age.** Khan Academy Kids' path offers activities by age, level and past performance (a store listing; that a child picks an age at setup was not confirmed). Netflix lets an account lock a profile with a PIN (older guides; current behavior unconfirmed). *Use:* age to start and play to adjust matches the first; Puddle Island chooses no lock at entry, because the cost of a toddler opening a grown-up's card is low once destructive actions are protected.

Sources, all excerpts: [Quantic Foundry on age (PC Gamer)](https://pcgamer.com/study-analyses-gaming-tastes-as-we-age), [what is a cozy game](https://playingthisweek.substack.com/p/what-is-a-cozy-game), [cozy games and who plays them (Eludamos)](https://eludamos.org/index.php/eludamos/article/view/7938), [YPulse tween gaming](https://www.ypulse.com/report/2026/06/30/tweens-gaming/), [Khan Academy Kids](https://www.commonsense.org/education/app/khan-academy-kids), [Netflix profile lock](https://www.techradar.com/news/add-parental-controls-to-your-netflix-profile-with-a-pin-heres-how).

### The gap review: what the registry showed

Read from each game's `bands`, `region` and level table on 2026-10-09 (counts of games live in the roadmap and README, not here):

- **Wonder Woods mostly replays the top levels of older games.** Lemonade Stand is the only game that begins in the `school` band, and fourteen more begin at pre-K. Of the games that reach it, 23 give the band only one or two levels (Bubble Pop, Jelly Drums, Pattern Train, Memory Match, Letter Trails, Song Maker, Size Parade and others). A game designed for ages 6–8 is the gap; [deepening short ladders](../ROADMAP.md#next-up) closes only part of it.
- **Thin cells in the subject-by-band grid.** At school: Bubble Beach has one game, Barnyard and Rainbow Meadow two each, Treehouse three (all creative toys). At lap: Puzzle Peaks has one (Puzzle Pals). Treehouse (art) and Bubble Beach (senses) have four games each and Music Mountain five, the smallest subjects.
- **Ages 8–11 have no band.** Each 8–11 proposal below is tagged either *fits the school band* (a school ladder has room to about age 9) or *needs the band and zone decision* (the roadmap's E1 zone-identity item and the band audit in [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md)).
- **The best-tested puzzles are couch-only.** Sudoku Garden, Lantern Lights, Picture Logic, Word Search, Island Bridges and Pond Conga have solvers, generators, hints and rule tests, but live in `GROWNUP_GAMES`, so the island cannot list them.
- **Reading and writing stop early.** Letter Trails traces capitals and names; Word Monsters ends at three-letter words and word families; every prompt in the catalog is spoken. No game asks her to read a printed word for its meaning, or to write a numeral or a lowercase letter.
- **Time stops at the clock.** Clock Tower is the only time game. No game names days, months or seasons or shows a life cycle.
- **The lap band has no song she already knows.** Song Maker generates random songs from a seed.

### Secret Code: detective doors (2026-10-10)

School level 7 appends two seeded doors with two or three prior guesses, none already correct, identifying exactly one distinct-color code. Existing stone taps, clear-slot taps and the key carry the round. Original clues stay visible; guesses contradicting the whole evidence count once, with a spoken reminder and faint solution stones after two misses. The ghost finger and controller bot reason from the same candidate rules. Levels 1–6 and saved pins retain their meanings; pre-K stays at 1–3, school extends to 2–7.

## Egg Catch: one setting for two eggs (2026-10-10)

School level 7 appends “Set it once”: brown enters the top fork; white enters the opposite branch from a second hen. Three seeded pairs ask for one arrangement before either rolls. A green arrow releases both paths one at a time, with gates locked. Unsuccessful pairs stay for revision, count one miss per attempt and give spoken guidance; two attempts show the needed directions. Boards are dealt unsolved with exactly one solution, and hint steps and ghost-finger taps use the tested rules. The school window is 4–7; saved levels, pins and couch course levels keep their meaning. Scoped how-to support, speech, browser coverage and inventory were extended with the level.

## Light Lab: where will it shine? (2026-10-10)

School level 7 locks the mirrors for three endpoint predictions, then unlocks them for a fourth planning puzzle. Prediction choices are actual reachable rock or edge endpoints, with three distinct 100-unit targets on each generated board. A wrong prediction reveals the beam and its endpoint without adding a miss or hint. The hint and ghost finger use the same endpoint rules; the couch bot uses the same choices through its focus ring. Existing school levels and saved level numbers stay in place.

- `npm run typecheck` and 30 focused Light Lab, how-to and registry tests passed; 200 seeded boards checked distinct endpoints, the actual endpoint and the plan solution.
- `BROWSER_SUITE=woods WOODS_ONLY=light FROM_LEVEL=7` passed with a wrong unscored prediction and the final plan. `BROWSER_SUITE=fingerdemo FINGER_ONLY=light-lab FINGER_LEVELS=7` finished cleanly in 17.1 seconds. The prediction screenshot was reviewed.
- Device speech and iPad target size remain open in the roadmap.

## Read & Do first slice (2026-10-10)

Built the gap-review game's first three levels in Story Grove: color words with hats (pre-K 1), big/small duck phrases (pre-K 2), then duck/cat jump/sleep sentences (school 3; school range 1–3). Each seeded round covers its whole pool exactly once, with shuffled choices and a single acceptable answer. Printed words and a speaker supply free replayable speech; the sign is not automatically read. A wrong choice shows and speaks its meaning, then replays the requested sign word by word. Two misses on one sign add one persistent glow hint. Every pool ends through the shell with one sticker, with help or without. The ghost finger plays real taps at every level. No save or shared engine changes. The actual speech, child comprehension and independent reading remain checks for a person.

The original sketch, preserved from the idea notebook:

| **Read & Do / pre-K–school** | Read a sign and make the scene do it ("the duck can jump"); tap any word to hear it | One sight word and a picture, then two-word phrases, then a sentence and an action. Hazel the squirrel can post the signs | M–L · new (sharpens "building a sentence") |

**Read & Do.** *Fun action:* read a sign and make the scene do it. *Skill:* recognising common words by sight, then reading a short sentence. *Smallest round:* one sight word beside a picture ("red", choose the red hat), then "big duck", then "the duck can jump". *Support:* every word replays its speech when tapped (replaying is free and never a hint); a wrong action plays out what the sentence would mean and then re-reads it word by word. *Deeper play:* two actions in order, a sentence with a question, then a short story. *Word source:* the Dolch lists, 40 pre-primer words and 52 primer words, are the natural ladder; the colors, numbers and directions already in the art cover many of them, and the three-letter nouns Word Monsters teaches supply the rest. *Host alternative:* none; no game asks her to read. *Checks:* AGENTS asks for a check of the actual device speech before claiming a phonics skill, and the same care applies to words read aloud here; reading progress is its own level and never forces harder number work (the E2 note in the [roadmap](../ROADMAP.md#e2-middle-elementary)).


### Fill and Dump pour mode (2026-10-10)

Preschool opens levels 1–5. Level 5 tips a jug by pulling right: displacement pours water into a cup, lifting stops, and overflow stays in a spill tray. Three cups use half, quarter and three-quarter lines, first half then a seeded order. A six-percent cup tolerance accepts the check. Tap the cup to empty into the tray, then the tray to recover water. All water is conserved and retries stay free; only an incorrect nonempty check is a miss, with one glow hint per cup after two misses. Three cups finish with one sticker. The touch demonstration uses the same pour and recovery controls.

Rules cover seeded targets, reachable completion, conservation, overflow, recovery, tolerance and miss/hint accounting. Custom pointer handling locks one finger and stops on cancellation, lost release, leaving the view or resize. Capacity comparison and the Quick Tricks pour show remain proposals; no unused shared infrastructure was added. Device touch feel, spoken output and child comprehension stay open in the roadmap.

Story Steps now has ten stories of four pictures each: the sandcastle, the sandwich, an apple tree, washing the dog, a rainy day and getting ready for bed join the flower, tower, snow friend and butterfly. Each level draws from its own pool (levels 1–2 build-up stories, level 4 change-over-time stories, 5–7 all ten), and extra pictures never come from a lookalike of the round's story (stack: tower, snow, sandcastle, sandwich; seed: flower, apple); level 7's two extras come from two different families. See [the deep dive](../deep-dives/story-steps.md#build-slices-in-order), slice 1.

## Puzzle Pals scene data and six new scenes (2026-10-11)

[PR 137](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/137) builds deep-dive slice 2. The scenes are now Pixi-free data in `src/games/puzzle-pals/scenes.ts` (id, animal, sound, spoken name, sky, ground, hills, cloud, sun or moon or neither) and six are new: a pig in the mud, a cow in the snow, a bunny in the autumn leaves, a cat at night, a bear at a picnic and a dog in the rain, with trees, a snowman, snowflakes, a cottage and lamp, stars, a blanket with fruit, a doghouse and rain drawn in code. A round draws any of the twelve. `STICKER_SCENES` is the first six and `stickerScene(seed)` is what the sticker and the map icon use, so a saved sticker keeps its picture; a test pins seed to scene for seeds 1 to 200 from a list recorded before the change, and the six original pictures were compared pixel by pixel with the old code (36 draws, no differing pixel). `pictureScene` also returns every part of the picture with the box it fills; a test is an empty-piece guard for the new scenes: every piece of every grid overlaps some part by more than a sliver and no two pieces overlap the same set of parts (the friend counts as nine parts). It scores bounding boxes, so it counts the plain-sky corners of the friend's box and sky details that sit behind its head; it does not show that the pieces look different. A test also checks that each picture differs in sky or ground from at least two others, so level 9's two pictures in one tray can be sorted.

Not done, and still slice 1: the fairness of all twelve scenes, which needs a finer check than boxes (see the deep dive's fairness slice) and must run over the new six as well as the originals. The box test flags bunny and dog (a middle piece holds nothing) and a test records that gap so a fix has to take them off the list; it does not see the duck's near-blank sky pieces. The box lid, flat sides and spatial hints are untouched.

Drag the cause before it; wrong choices receive a spoken action and gentle retry, two misses glow the correct cause, and completion tells the pair with “because.” Reuses the card drag, replay, completion and ghost finger. ## Orchard Rows first slice (2026-10-11)

Built `orchard-rows` in Counting Cove for school, levels 1–3 ([PR #140](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/140)). Fun action: a whole row sprouts fruit trees on touch-down. Learning purpose: equal groups in a rectangular array, counting by rows and by columns, and finding the size of a missing row. Two different seeded orchards form one complete round. The first level plants 2×3, 3×2 or 3×3 and counts groups with running addition and speech. Level 2 plants 2×4, 3×4 or 2×5, turns the same orchard and counts its columns, preserving the total. Level 3 starts with one row absent from 3×5, 4×5, 5×4 or 5×5, asks how many trees complete it, then plants and counts both ways. Completion through the shell awards one sticker, with help or without.

Starting support: drawn trees and empty planting holes, spoken instructions replayed by the guide, spoken group counts, running addition, and freely replayable row counts. Planting/counting in any order and repeating rows are exploration, never misses. A wrong number choice gets a boing and a spoken explanation; the second on that question glows its answer once and counts one hint. For example, choosing the row count instead of its tree count says every row needs the same number as the full row. All touch controls are unscaled: 100-unit row bands and 120×110 number/turn buttons. The bot uses these same controls.

This earns a new game: Lasso Loops makes equal groups through capture and Little Helpers distributes objects; here the rectangular arrangement and a change of viewpoint are the actual action. Garden Rows is a row/column uniqueness puzzle. No existing game's levels or saved IDs moved, and no new save fields were needed. Co-play and an off-screen blocks activity are supplied. First/last plays, portrait review, demos and rules are recorded in [VERIFICATION](../VERIFICATION.md); actual iPad speech, touch and child understanding are still open. Larger/place-value arrays remain outside this slice.

## Pattern Train slice 1: the train ends anywhere (2026-10-11)

The first build slice of the [deep dive](../deep-dives/pattern-train.md#build-slices-in-order), taking the developer-table lean to fix the levels before adding any ([PR 141](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/141)). `buildTrain` now shows two full repeats plus 0 to unit−1 more cars before the empty one (two empty cars start at slot 0 or 1), so trains run 5–9 cars and the answer is no longer always the first car's piece; level 6's gap is anywhere but a repeat's first car, in either repeat. Level 12 starts at a multiple of its step that is not the step, keeps every number to 100, hides any car from the third on, and draws two wrong numbers from a step back, a step on and one off, shuffled. Levels 10 and 11 keep their seven-car train (their lines name the seventh car) until slices 2 and 3. School's range now follows the plan table, the how-to's last step is bounded to level 12, and `pattern.number` asks for the glowing car. No level, pin or saved ID moved; couch stops (4, 6, 9) get the new boards, and no course uses Pattern Train. The pinned-sticker item is moot: the sticker is one fixed train with no seed or list. Checks in [VERIFICATION](../VERIFICATION.md).

## Number Trails, first slice (2026-10-13)

[PR #241](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/241): `number-trails`, its own game (the Letter Trails deep dive's lean), in Counting Cove for preschool 1–2 and pre-K 2–3. It imports Letter Trails' `sampleStroke`, `advanceTrace` and `traceWaypoints` and adds `digits.ts` (0 to 9, every stroke starting at the top; two strokes for 4, 5 and 9). A green ring and an arrow mark each stroke's start. Level 1 draws 1 to 5, level 2 all ten numerals, level 3 makes her wake exactly that many sleeping ducks, apples or stars from a group three larger. The numeral forms are an interim convention the developer chose (simple common forms), unchecked against a publisher's chart. The sketch it came from:

**Number Trails.** *Deep dive stance (2026-10-11, [Letter Trails](../deep-dives/letter-trails.md#decisions-for-the-developer) decision 5):* lean toward its own game that shares Letter Trails' stroke module; the letter ladder builds no digit levels. *Fun action:* follow the firefly along a numeral. *Skill:* forming 0 to 9 in stroke order, with the quantity beside it. *Smallest round:* trace 1 to 5 and see that many things. *Support:* a start dot and an arrow at the beginning of each stroke, because the reversed numerals are usually 2, 3, 5 and 7 and children confuse 6 and 9; the existing ordered checkpoints already teach the movement rather than the shape. *Deeper play:* 0 to 9, a numeral in order, "make the number of ducks". *Host:* a mode of Letter Trails; its `LETTERS` table is a map of strokes in a 0 to 1 box and `advanceTrace` is shape-agnostic, so digits are a sibling table plus pictures. Lowercase is larger: it needs a four-line frame (ascender, x-height, descender). Pick one stroke convention (Handwriting Without Tears, D'Nealian and Zaner-Bloser differ) and check it against the publisher's chart before encoding.

## Block Tower prediction replay and authored pairs (2026-10-12)

[PR #244](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/244), branch `codex/block-tower-prediction`, dispatch `block-tower-prediction-20261013T045450Z`. Level 5 only: three unscored observations, frozen Watch again, explicit Continue and one shell sticker. Existing bands/IDs/pins and sticker snapshots preserved; no reach, new levels, storage or decisions changed. Actual speech, iPad input and child fit remain open.

   **One observation, replay and completion.** Use the states held → releasing/explaining → result. In held, tapping either tower marks that prediction and releases both ropes once. Lock tower input and result controls through the animation/spoken explanation; extra taps and cancelled presses do not queue a release. Show the fallen outlines, the unsupported group's middle and its supporting edge, plus support on the survivor. Describe what happened regardless of which tower was picked; do not require a correct prediction or a verbal answer.

   At result, keep the evidence visible and offer two separate ≥100-unit controls with pictures, touch-down feedback and replayable spoken labels: **Watch again** and **Continue**. Watch again restores the original held geometry internally and repeats the same release/explanation, retaining the original prediction marker; it does not ask for another answer. Freeze pair ID, positions, direction, side, colors and outcome for the entire observation. Replay consumes no puzzle/sticker RNG, advances no task and writes no progress. It can be repeated freely. Continue is available after the first complete explanation; it records that observation once, clears its evidence and opens the next held pair. Continue after observation three calls the normal round finish once. Replay of the third remains possible before Continue. There is no auto-advance, timeout or mandatory replay. A duplicate Continue, a late replay callback, resize or leaving during release cannot create another observation, completion or sticker. Scene-owned timing/voice and destruction guards must enforce this. Leaving uses the existing home route and does not save an incomplete observation.

   **Bounded authored pool.** Author seven canonical pairs below, all three blocks, with middles listed bottom-up in eighths of width (`W = 8`), floor support (`edge = null`) and positive lean. All canonical data and mirrored/side variants are validated by automated rule tests. The falling index is the lowest unsupported block (0-based); equality at an edge still falls. Mirror the whole pair by negating x, and swap the two display sides without changing either outcome. Both towers always lean the same way.

   | Pool / ID | Standing tower | Falling tower | Reach difference / falling index | Intended contrast |
   | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
   | Clear C1 | `[0,1,2]` | `[0,3,6]` | 4 / 1 | Upper group's middle passes the lower block's edge |
   | Clear C2 | `[0,2,3]` | `[0,2,6]` | 3 / 1 | Same middle-block position; longer top moves the group to the edge |
   | Clear C3 | `[0,1,3]` | `[0,1,6]` | 3 / 2 | Same middle-block position; the top itself loses support |
   | Matched M1 | `[0,2,5]` | `[0,1,5]` | 0 / 2 | Equal top reach; top's support differs |
   | Matched M2 | `[0,2,5]` | `[0,3,5]` | 0 / 1 | Equal top reach; upper group's support differs |
   | Matched M3 | `[0,2,4]` | `[0,0,4]` | 0 / 2 | Equal top reach; top lies exactly on its support edge |
   | Matched M4 | `[0,3,4]` | `[0,4,4]` | 0 / 1 | Equal top reach; upper group lies exactly on its support edge |

   Select the run's three immutable pairs with supplied seeded RNG in bounded work, before play: pre-K uses C1–C3 in shuffled order; school uses two distinct clear IDs followed by one matched ID. Shuffle finite pools rather than rerolling. Select orientation and survivor side independently of geometry, with a shuffled two-value bag plus one seeded extra value for each run: a three-observation run has one/two of each, never all one side or direction. Exact 50/50 is impossible within three; the complete canonical variant pool (each ID × two directions × two survivor sides, 28 variants) must be exactly balanced. Do not promise all seven IDs in one run or persistent cross-session deck coverage; repeated sessions may repeat a pair. Give both towers the same bottom-to-top color sequence, selected independently of the answer, with no pre-release green/red cue. Freeze all presentation choices on replay. Pin seed-to-sticker art before changing puzzle RNG; code-drawn scenery must consume no puzzle draws. Keep original `makeStand` as an explicitly tested valid fallback, not a silent substitution that defeats authored coverage.

   **Pre-K fit and coverage boundary.** Proposed pre-K play uses visible reach contrasts throughout, with the same freely replayable explanation and no numerical center-of-mass calculation, reading or memory requirement. School's third pair removes the “longer top falls” shortcut: equal reach can have different support, and either the top alone or its upper group can fall. The finite pool covers those two failure locations, both edge equality and beyond-edge failure, both lean directions and survivor sides. It does not cover different masses/shapes, tabletop support or every valid three-block configuration. Automated solvability does not prove that a child notices the lines or understands the group. Pre-K's clear-only selection is a conservative proposed content choice, not evidence of readiness or a change to its band window. Keep the existing [person checks](../DEVICE-CHECKS.md#needs-a-person-or-a-device) open: actual device speech/target clearance, whether she chooses/replays/continues, and whether matched pairs are useful for pre-K. Any later choice to put matched pairs in pre-K needs that evidence and its own bounded content change. Decisions 1–4 above remain unresolved; this definition does not settle experiments at 6, ordering at 7–9, temporary build supports or co-play.

   **Build acceptance.** Keep pair data, selection and transitions inspectable in `logic.ts` where practical. Unit rules must validate all 28 variants: exactly one survivor, authored falling index/support witness, strict-edge outcomes, equal reach on M1–M4, same-way lean and no color answer. Test seeded/bounded selection including constant RNG, pre-K three clear IDs, school two distinct clear plus matched, side/direction one-or-two balance, frozen replay with no RNG draws, and Continue/completion idempotence. A deterministic replay must show the same result after repeat, resize and interrupted input. Update level-5 voice/how-to to explain Watch again, Continue and three observations, and teach `autotouch()` to predict through public input, wait for result and press Continue (including final completion); it must not bypass the new controls or loop on replay.

Tap a square (a given number can be chosen to look along its lines), then a number tile; Marks, Erase, Hint and Start over are tiles of the same tray, each at least 64 logical units, beside the bed on a wide view (the 9 by 9 bed is exactly 64 units on the smallest, 1024 by 768) or under it, bigger, on a tall one, so zoom and scroll were not needed. ## Word Search by touch (2026-10-13)

Lands stage D's first puzzle, built in [PR #255](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/255): all four levels play by touch on the island (`inputs: ['touch', 'controller']`, `touchDemo`). One pointer layer over the grid tells a tap from a slide (a finger that travels 14 units before lifting slides; a shorter touch is a tap and lights its square at once). A tap marks a first letter, a second checks the line (the same letter lets go; a letter off the line moves the first letter there, free). A grid that is all in view also takes a drag along the word. A grid bigger than its window (squares are never under 64 units) is clipped by a mask, slides under a drag, shows soft edge strips where more lies beyond, and a hint scrolls its ringed letter into view. Hint and Start over are 72-unit buttons; the word list sits beside the grid, or under it on a tall view. The ghost finger scrolls with a real traced drag; `GameContext.demo` cuts the demonstration to three words. The couch layout is untouched (`touch = !ctx.couch`, and a first `control()` call switches to it).

## Island Bridges by touch (2026-10-13)

Lands stage D's second slice, built in [PR #256](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/256): all four levels play by touch on the island (`inputs: ['touch', 'controller']`, `touchDemo`). Faint dots mark the water between two islands that face each other; tap it to lay a plank, again for a second (a third, a crossing or a full island bumps at no cost). **Take off** is a switch: while it is on a tap lifts a plank (free); a hint to take a plank off turns it on and lifting that bridge turns it off. Hint and Start over are buttons. A layer over the sea resolves the tap with `edgeAt` in `logic.ts` (the cells between two islands are that bridge's water, at least 64 logical units wide; in a cell shared by crossing bridges the nearer line wins, then the one with planks), beside the buttons on a wide view and above them, bigger, on a tall one; the 11 by 11 sea fits at exactly 64 on the smallest view, so neither zoom nor scroll was needed. `waterOf` and `headStart` serve the ghost finger and the how-to demonstration (the answer's last six planks left, via `GameContext.demo`). The couch layout, rules and accounting are unchanged (a plank beyond the answer is a miss, a hint counts), and a call to `control()` still switches the game to the controller layout. Checks: [verification entry](VERIFICATION-2026-10-07-13.md#2026-10-13--island-bridges-by-touch-lands-stage-d).

Existing level 6 deals one left edge and one right edge in seeded order: two blocks reaching four eighths, then three reaching five. Blocks remain bottom-first in canonical outward coordinates; table, drops, help, bot, star and cartoon fall project to the active side. Strict support, early success, top-only revision and one hint per task remain. The hint and drop ghost follow resize. The archived slice-5 build packet follows; its future-tense verification text describes the original definition, not current proof. Current checks are in VERIFICATION.md.

5. **Ready, defined; unbuilt: mirror reach content at existing 6** (definition 2026-10-13, `codex/block-tower-mirror-definition`, [PR #254](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/254)). Fun action: build out toward a star on either side of a table, watch the support result and revise the top block. Learning purpose: the same equal-block support rule works in either direction; the star's distance and a block's color do not change its weight. This docs-only definition preserves current accounting and needs none of the open decisions above. Dispatch `block-tower-mirror-definition-20261013T110552Z`; worker `01a134f1-36e0-7d63-9e78-3c390d8cb02d`.

   **Tasks and selection.** Stay at saved level 6, school window 5–6, with exactly two tasks in their original order: `(blocks: 2, star: 4)`, then `(blocks: 3, star: 5)`. `star` stays a positive distance in eighths of the original width (`W = 8`), measured beyond the active table edge. Shuffle the two directions `[right, left]` once with the supplied seeded RNG; attach them to those tasks and freeze them for the run. Thus the two possible runs are right/left and left/right; each task has a mirror variant across seeds, without four tasks or a harder distance. Resize, retries, hints and top-block revision neither redraw a direction nor advance a task. Keep equal widths/masses, original block colors, sticker seed drawings and one shell reward after two successes. Reaching with fewer than the available blocks is still accepted; this is not a minimum-block challenge.

   **Table and coordinate model.** Let `d = +1` mean a right edge with the table extending left, and `d = -1` mean a left edge with the table extending right. For screen edge `E` and block width `BW`, physical block middle `x = (screenX - E) * W / BW`; canonical outward middle `u = d * x`. Store/search the canonical middles bottom first, and project them with `screenX = E + d * u * BW / W`. A table is a half-plane in the rule model; its drawing must cover the inward drop domain without introducing a second unsupported edge. Keep the floor-supported (`edge = null`) rule used by level 5 unchanged.

   | Rule / geometry | Right (`d = +1`) | Left (`d = -1`) | Shared canonical rule |
   | --- | --- | --- | --- |
   | Table supports whole-stack mean `m` | `m < 0` | `m > 0` | Mean of `u` is strictly below 0; equality falls |
   | Star line from `E`, in eighths | `+4`, then `+5` | `-4`, then `-5` | Positive `star` stays 4, then 5 |
   | Outward face of a block at `x` reaches | `x + 4 >= star` | `x - 4 <= -star` | `u + 4 >= star`; line contact counts |
   | Accepted physical middle slots | Integers `-12..8` | Integers `-8..12` | The same 21 integer slots `-12..8` |

   For every block above the bottom, the mean of that block and all blocks above it must remain strictly within 4 eighths of the block below's middle. Reflection preserves that absolute-distance check and the lowest falling block index; never reverse the stack's bottom-to-top order. Test stability before reach, so a falling block touching the line cannot complete a task. For example, canonical `[-2, 0]` reaches 4 stably; `[-2, 0, 1]` reaches 5 with every placement stable. Their physical left-edge mirrors are `[2, 0]` and `[2, 0, -1]`.

   **Drops, solver and help.** Normalize pointer movement into `u` before the existing round-and-clamp operation, then project its result back to the active side. This also reflects rounding at half slots; independently rounding signed screen offsets would break symmetry. Reflect the existing forgiving horizontal acceptance region in canonical coordinates, retaining its vertical cutoff. Preview, committed drop, `finish`, `nextReach`, hint ghost and finger bot all use this one domain/projection. Search canonical slots in the existing descending order, requiring each prefix to stand immediately; use no final-state/scaffold search. A physical prefix `xs` at the right edge and its negation at the left edge must have the same failure index, reach result and completion existence, with each returned move reflected. Preserve `finish` results: `[]` for an already stable reach, `null` for an unstable or unfinishable prefix, otherwise a stable continuation. Help uses its first move; when no continuation exists, it highlights the actual top block to remove. Removing that block must return to a prefix with a completion, or allow another top removal until one exists. No hint may offer an unreachable slot.

   **Interaction, accounting and presentation.** Retain immediate settlement after each committed drop, free tray movement and top-only lifting; lock input through settlement/result, return cancelled or out-of-region drops to the tray, clear stale help on pickup and preserve resize/leave cleanup. A topple adds one miss; a full stable stack short of the line adds one miss; after two such failures in a task, constructive help counts once for that task. Further help does not recount it. Do not turn level 6 into unscored experiments or change adaptation, tall-glow timing on 3–4, or unscored level-5 predictions. Mirror the table, star line, placed blocks, drop/help ghosts, support explanation and fall direction together; lay out the tray and edge from `view`, keeping the pet and controls clear and all drag targets at least 100 logical units. The spoken instruction and level-6 how-to must work on either side (for example, “out past this edge”), with the same two-task finish. Device speech and whether a child understands the mirrored edge remain person checks.

   **Implementation exit evidence (not run for this definition).** Use the new-interaction tier: typecheck and unit tests proving both side orders/unchanged targets, exact mirror support and strict edge cases, reach-line equality, reflected half-slot rounding/clamping, and solver/hint/bot symmetry for every offered prefix up to each task's block budget. Include stable completions at every placement, impossible-prefix top-removal recovery and early success. One filtered level-6 `shortlist SHORTLIST_ONLY=tower FROM_LEVEL=6 TO_LEVEL=6` play should cover both side orders, one topple and one full-short miss, one counted hint, top recovery, cancellation/settling/resize, and exactly one saved result/sticker. Run `fingerdemo FINGER_ONLY=block-tower FINGER_LEVELS=6` for both orders, and review one portrait screenshot with the left edge and pet clearance; build only if shared files change. Do not add world/couch/offline runs for this slice. This definition changes only this dive, its tracker, verification and queue; no source, scripts, assets, tests, build or browser work, levels/bands, storage, temporary ropes, couch support or developer decisions are included.

## Pyramid Solitaire first slice (2026-10-13)

[PR #282](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/282), claim `0db5c75`, base `56132bc` plus working diff; [definition](../definitions/pyramid-solitaire.md). `pyramid-solitaire` is a grown-up touch/mouse game in Lantern Lake: lift exposed pairs summing to thirteen, or kings alone, to clear three finite face-up pyramids (3/4/5 rows, 6/10/15 cards). Both covering cards must be removed to expose a card. Seeded rank assignment follows a legal clearing order; exact mask search checks alternative routes. A mismatched pair counts a miss, two consecutive misses give counted help, and covered taps and legal dead ends are exploration. Hint recovers the nearest solvable ancestor when needed; a visible hint counts once. Undo and Start over preserve support and cost nothing. One shell sticker, replayable spoken support, level-scoped how-to and a real-tap ghost finger complete the slice.

Typecheck/build, 1,234 unit tests, 49 focused rules/catalog tests, filtered first/last play and demos passed; [verification](VERIFICATION-2026-10-13.md#2026-10-13--pyramid-solitaire-first-slice-and-local-build-audit). No stock, conventional deck restriction, controller/couch course or unfinished-deal save. These remain separate proposals; iPad comfort, device speech and grown-up enjoyment remain [open](../DEVICE-CHECKS.md#needs-a-person-or-a-device). The recently finished Snowball Merge, Marble Jump and Log Roll builds are recorded separately in [the local-build audit](LOCAL-GAME-BUILDS-2026-10-13.md), awaiting integration and excluded from this inventory.

On the island the first key or pad press switches their layout to the controller one (Bouncy Launch's spring squashes by power; Ferry Jam's and Penguin Slide's round undo buttons hide, Ferry Jam's focus ring shows) and `touchReturned()` restores touch at the next finger (the spring springs back up, even after a mid-air hop; Ferry Jam puts a key-held boat back first). ## The Woodland Picnic, the second Island Errand (2026-10-16)

[PR #479](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/479), `claude/woodland-picnic`, built from its [definition](../definitions/woodland-picnic.md) (chunk 22, W1). For the school band, from Wonder Woods: Hazel the squirrel postkeeper asks three jobs in any order, each a round of a game she knows at the story's level without moving the game's own level: invitations (Mail Carrier 6), baskets (Seesaw Balance 7) and the beat (Beat Builder 3, on its picnic stage). Each finished round changes the clearing (flags on three houses and lit stepping stones; two baskets on a carrying pole; a drum and bell on a stump). With all three done the friends (bear, bunny, duck) walk in along the stones, a stated eight-step picnic beat (`PICNIC_BEAT`, drum and pentatonic bells) plays twice, and Hazel's photo is kept once (`store.endStory('woodland')`). Once kept, a photo button opens it large with a green "tell it again" (`retellStory`; the keepsake stays). Home goes to Wonder Woods. Entry is the first stop on the Wonder Woods path (`StorySpot`) and a "Woodland Picnic" card on the subject view, for band `school` only.

Contract: `WOODLAND_STEPS`, `STORY_STEPS`, `StoryId` and `StoryStep` in `src/content/world.ts`; `stories.woodland` in the save with a default and repair in `migrate()` (an old save gets an empty telling); the store's `completeStep`, `endStory` and `retellStory` take a story ID (default `'picnic'`); `StoryRound` in `App.ts` carries an optional story; route `go.woodland(from?)`. New: `WoodlandScene.ts`, `src/art/woodland.ts`, `src/content/woodland.ts` (and tests), `woodland.*` voice lines. The Windy Picnic is unchanged. Checks are in [VERIFICATION.md](VERIFICATION-2026-10-16.md#2026-10-16--woodland-picnic).

## Dot Link level 11, Find my buddy (2026-10-17)

[PR #524](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/524), `claude/dot-link-buddy`, slice 2 of the [dive](../deep-dives/dot-link.md#build-slices-in-order). A toddler entry on a 3×3 two-color board: one adjacent same-color pair, chosen by seeded `pickBuddy` among all such pairs, wiggles from the first second. Drawing between them (either direction, or a longer line through both) pops only that pair, and a new pair lights after the refill. A full two-color 3×3 always has at least two same-color neighbor pairs, so the dive's "exactly one pair" is built as exactly one pair lit; the goal is 4 buddies (the dive gave none; level 2 uses 6 links). Another same-color line shakes, does not pop, boings, says `dots.buddyhint` and counts one miss; a cross-color drag does nothing. The pulse is not a hint, so there is no idle or two-miss hint and hints stay 0. Toddler band `{ min: 1, max: 11, order: [1, 11, 2] }` with `entryLevels: [11]`; the board scales up to 1.4× for a catch area of about 137 units per dot. New voice lines `dots.buddy` and `dots.buddyhint`; the how-to gains a level 11 step and the square step is now bounded to level 5 (open-ended `from: 5` would have claimed levels 6 and 11). Browser: `BATCH_ONLY=dots` now runs levels 1–6 and 11, with `DOTS_LEVELS=11`. Checks in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-17.md#2026-10-17--dot-link-level-11-find-my-buddy); open: a toddler's drag on a device.

## Rainbow Fingers slice 2, every touch counts, pots, nudge (2026-10-17)

[PR #537](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/537), `claude/rainbow-fingers-every-touch`, slice 2 of the [dive](../deep-dives/rainbow-fingers.md#build-slices-in-order). Levels 1–2: every lifted touch counts toward the hang-it-up frame (pat, hold or drag; `touchKind`, `strokesCounted`, `frameShows` in `logic.ts`), five touches or one and 25 s, and a pat splats a palm-sized flower (`SPLAT_RADIUS` 50). Pots have hit circles of 52 (104 across) at spacing 104; `potLayout` keeps them clear of the pet and wraps to two rows only when one will not fit (every real view is at least 1024 wide, so seven fit in one row). Each color pot carries an ink mark (`POT_MARKS`: red dot, orange stripe, yellow star, green heart, blue swirl, purple ring). After 8 quiet seconds (`NUDGE_AFTER`) a blank spot sparkles and the pet hops, never a hint. Fixes gaps 2, 3 and 9. Hint accounting (decision 5) is not built. How-to card levels 1–2 and the `early` paint case updated.

## Little Conductor levels 1–2, music M2 (2026-10-20)

Branch `claude/little-conductor-m2`, slice M2 of the [study](../deep-dives/subjects/music-instruments.md#build-slices-in-order), from the [definition](../definitions/little-conductor.md). New game `little-conductor` in Music Mountain (the land now holds 6): toddler 1–2, preschool 1–2, pre-K 2–2 (capped at 2 until M3). Three seeded friends (not the bear or pip) on a bandstand play one seeded four-beat pentatonic motif (hop, slide or wave) on the game's own score clock (`advance` in `logic.ts`); the turtle pad is 60 bpm, the rabbit 90. The first pad tap starts the band at once; later changes wait for the next beat, with a pending ring and clock icon, and a tap on the playing pad calls off a waiting change. Four beat marks sit under the active pad; level 2 shows a check beside each pad heard whole. Done is dim until the heard gate (level 1: a whole phrase at the pace last chosen; level 2: both paces heard whole and the chosen one whole since chosen; a waiting change closes the gate at once). An early Done blinks the unlit marks and speaks hear-whole or hear-other, never counted. Listen again restarts the tune without resetting heard marks. At the gate Done plays the tune once more with claps, the band bows, and the round finishes with 0 misses and 0 hints and one sticker. Six idle seconds before the first tap pulse the turtle pad once (never a hint); the band plays at half gain while the pet speaks. `music: silenced(STYLES.jelly)`, added to the registry test's music-making list; new `sfx.pluck(step, gain)` and an optional gain on `sfx.drum`. The ghost-finger bot taps turtle, rabbit once turtle is heard whole, then Done (both levels). Nothing is saved; no couch version. Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-20.md#2026-10-20--little-conductor-levels-12-music-m2).

## Jar Sort levels 1–8 (2026-10-20)

New game `jar-sort` in Rainbow Meadow (8 games), region puzzle-peaks, music `STYLES.paint`, skills planning/sorting/spatial-reasoning; preschool 1–3, pre-K 2–5, school 4–8. Tap a jar to lift its top fruit and another to drop it (same fruit on top, or an empty jar, with room; one piece per move); a drag from jar to jar does the same; the ground or the lifted jar sets it back. A refused move is free at levels 1–2 (lit target jars) and one miss from 3; every second miss on a board glows the hint move (one hint); a wander hint after `max(8, 2 × par)` legal moves; the hint search is capped at 200,000 states with an undo fallback; a stuck board makes the pet say so and pulses undo, never a miss or hint. Level 8 lids only on a fruit's own mat. Free unlimited undo. A ghost finger taps along the solver's path and the demonstration plays one board. No couch version. Deviations from the definition: the jars slide off screen at the end (the land's host does not carry them); the portrait row arithmetic assumed a 768-wide view, but the upright logical view is 1024 wide, so seven jars use two rows as in landscape; the card's level-3-on line reads "a fruit dropped where it cannot go counts as a mistake" (the card test forbids the word "level"). ## Egg Catch, roll when ready at levels 4–5 (2026-10-20)

Levels 4 (route) and 5 (sort) no longer roll the egg on a clock (it was 3.5 s for the first egg, 2 s after). The egg waits at the top (`held`) until she taps the green arrow, the same top-right button level 7 uses; gates can still be flipped while it rolls, a wrong-way egg is still a miss (`egg.wrongway`), and after two misses the needed gates glow as before and the arrow now also pulses once the gates are set (levels 4, 5 and 7). `routeTouch(gates, exit)` in `logic.ts` gives the ghost finger its moves: flip the needed gates, then tap the arrow; a rule test checks at most two flips and then 'go', with the egg reaching the exit. Voice: `egg.route` and `egg.sort` end with "then tap the green arrow". How-to: steps 4 and 5 name the arrow, with a new note for levels 4–5. The `eggs` case in `scripts/browser-check.mjs` honours `TO_LEVEL`, waits for the held egg, checks it is still waiting after 4.5 s, sets the gates and taps the arrow. ## Egg Catch, a miss that teaches and hens that wait at levels 2–3 (2026-10-21)

[PR #720](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/720), `claude/egg-catch-miss-teaches`, from the [deep dive](../deep-dives/egg-catch.md) slice 2. At levels 2–3 a missed brown egg (one someone was playing over) gets `sfx.boing()` and the new line `egg.peep` "Peep! Slide under the next one."; a white egg caught in the basket boings too. After two misses (the existing hint, which still slows the fall 1.4×) the hay under the next brown egg's lane glows (a yellow ellipse and ring above the hay piles, `eventMode` none); `glowLane()` in `logic.ts` picks the brown egg in the air that lands first, else the next brown egg in the seeded stream.

- **Hens that wait.** `HenPace` in `logic.ts`: hens lay only while someone plays (touch-down, a steering move, a finger held still, or couch stick/button input through `control()`). An egg that lands with nobody playing since it was laid settles the hens (sleepy look), is not a miss and gets no line; the next touch wakes them after 0.6 s. At level 3 a second egg in the air also waits for play since the last egg, so a round left alone lays one egg and stops. Known consequence: a child who stays perfectly still under the right lane also settles the hens after that caught egg and the next touch resumes; this follows the dive's literal rule ("no touch since it was laid").
- **Bots.** `autotouch` touches once per new egg where the basket already is (and to wake settled hens); the couch `autoplay` gives a small stick nudge for the same; `HenPace.owesPlay` exposes it.
- **How-to.** Level 2–3 note: "After two missed eggs, the hay under the next brown egg glows and the eggs fall slower. The hens rest when nobody is playing and lay again at the next touch."
- **Tests.** An idle round lays one egg with no miss at levels 2 and 3; busy play keeps laying with no unplayed landings; a mid-round look-away costs at most one (level 2) or two (level 3) unplayed eggs and the next touch wakes the hens; the second egg waits for play; the glow lane equals the next brown egg over 100 seeds at both levels.

## Block Garden levels 1–8 (2026-10-20)

[PR #655](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/655), `claude/block-garden`, built from its [definition](../definitions/block-garden.md). New game `block-garden` in Rainbow Meadow (now 9 games), region puzzle-peaks, music `STYLES.paint`; pre-K 1–3, school 2–6, grown-up 5–8. Drag fixed seed-tray shapes onto a bed; full rows and columns bloom and empty until every bud blooms (level 8 empties the bed). Rules, solver and generators are in `src/games/block-garden/logic.ts`; frozen gardens (14 per level, 5–8) are in `puzzles.ts`, written by `scripts/block-garden-gardens.mjs`. Touch only, with an `autotouch` drag bot, voice lines `block-garden.*` and `game.block-garden`, a how-to card, and the `blockgarden` browser case. Built on every default in the definition's open decisions. Deviations from the definition:

- **Generation.** Levels 1–4 are built at runtime from per-level templates (one straight gap; a crossing row and column each one domino short; a corner-shaped crossing gap with an optional third row that can only bloom after the column empties; two rows with straight gaps plus a decoy), each checked by the solver and the level filter, 50 tries and then an authored fallback (never needed over 500 seeds in a test). Levels 5–8 are frozen from block templates (a block of rows full of stones but for the planned pieces), solver- and filter-checked. A round shows its garden under one of the square's eight symmetries rather than a left–right mirror; par is kept (tested).
- **Search cap.** It counts states generated (each placement tried), `SEARCH_CAP` 30,000, not 200,000 visited states: an IDA* search with a squares-needed lower bound and a tray-aware placement bound. The worst level-8 off-plan search measured about 320 ms on Node (the definition's target was under 150 ms; an iPad check is open). While she follows the stored solution the hint reads it without searching; past the cap it steps back to her latest state on the stored solution. After each planting a 4,000-state search checks for a dead end.
- **How-to note.** The level-3+ line reads "A shape dropped where it cannot fit counts as a mistake." (a test forbids level talk).

Finite solvable gardens (built backward at 1–4, a frozen solver-checked list at 5–8), fixed shapes with no rotation, every bud blooms (or the bed is emptied), free undo instead of a reshuffle; a controller cursor, box clears and an opt-in endless run stay later modes | M |

[PR #669](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/669), `claude/shape-sorter-support-truth`, [deep dive](../deep-dives/shape-sorter.md) build slice 1 (gaps 2, 4, 6 and 8). We're looking for the shape with …"), said every time; it is still one miss and bounces (decision 3 default). `CLASSIC` pins levels 7–8 to the six classic kinds instead of `SHAPES`; `boxTopFor`, `BOX_FRONT` and `FLOOR_AT` in `logic.ts` with a test that every plan's lid stands on the floor, at least 40 from the top and inside the width, in four views. IDs, windows and the dead `lap` row unchanged (slice 2 handles lap). `scripts/browser-check.mjs` `originals` shapes case: level 1 checks that a box tap, a floor wiggle and a far drop are free; level 8 drops a fitting but unasked shape first (one miss, it comes back), then the asked hole (glow).

### Feelings Faces slice 1: pools, the pet's own hug and scenery that answers (2026-10-20, [PR #673](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/673))

Level 6 `EVENTS` 4 → 8 (rain → sad, butterfly → surprised landing clear of the face, lullaby → sleepy with falling bell notes and no night overlay, ball → happy); a round draws four different happenings. Level 8 `CARE_SCENARIOS` 2 → 5: balloon → hug, moon → pillow, plus `CARE_ONLY` happenings that never appear at level 6 (ice cream off a cone → sad → hug, snow → cold → scarf, empty picnic basket → hungry → apple). Deviation from the dive: it said 4 scenarios with a test of four distinct helpers, which is impossible because balloon and ice cream are both hug; the dive's optional picnic basket was added, so the five cover every helper and a round shows four different happenings and at least three helpers. `FRIENDS` 6 → 8 (pip, duck) for level 7 and the sticker. Voice: `feel.hug` is the pet's own preference ("{pet} loves a hug!" / "A big hug! {pet} likes that."), `feel.need-sad`, `feel.helped` and second variants for `feel.that`/`feel.how`, seven `feel.event-*` lines. A tap on a happening prop wobbles it (rotation driven from update, so an event's tween survives) and replays its sound, never a miss; props draw in a `front` layer above the big pet; clearProps kills a prop's tweens before destroying it. How-to: friends step scoped to level 7, new "Tap the pet to hear the question again." step at 2–6 and 8, finish per level (lap level 1 "Free play ends by itself after a few taps, with a sticker.", from 2 "Four questions, then a sticker."). `scripts/browser-check.mjs` `feelings8` takes `FEELINGS_LEVEL=6|8`.

How-to (`src/content/howto.ts`): level 3's step no longer offers a take-out (a seed goes in and grows at once), the level 5 and 7 steps are capped (`to: 5`, `to: 7`), and level 1 says each bed grows by itself; the bounded-ladder test in `howto.test.ts` now includes `garden-grow`. New voice line `grow.full` ("Every bed has a seed! Tap the cloud to make it rain, or tap a seed to take it out.") is spoken with the boing when a packet is tapped with every bed planted (levels 4-7); not a miss. Taking a seed out (4-7) recounts aloud with `count`. Level 1 has no global lock while a flower grows: other empty beds plant at once and the round ends only when no bed is still growing (a `growing` counter in `checkFull`). The co-play tip names the weather seat ('One of you plants, one is the weather: tap the cloud. Say "Rain, rain... it grows!"'). Lesson: a first three-line tip covered the cloud at the top centre and swallowed the scripted cloud tap at level 4 (the toddler band shows the tip), so a co-play tip at the top must stay about two lines and not cover top-centre controls. Not done: level 7's drawn colors (decision 4).

Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-20.md#2026-10-20--garden-grow-true-how-to-and-spoken-gaps-slice-1).

## Stop and Go: every touch counts, slice 1 (2026-10-20)

[PR #678](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/678), `claude/stop-and-go-every-touch`. Level 1 (toy): a tap on the car toots a two-note horn and hops it (on green it also drives round) and counts as one of the round's eight touches, like a light tap; the toy light going red squashes the waiting car. Level 2 (send): a tap on a car behind the front one toots and hops, neither a send nor a miss; yellow on the front car toots with the spoken nudge; red squashes the car and is the miss. Levels 2 and 3: the hint glow (after two misses) rings the front car or the feet button, and only while the light is green (it was the light); level 3 now clears misses and the glow after a good step, as level 2 does after a send. Cars rumble with a small bob on red, and on green a tick sounds and every waiting car blinks its headlight. New line `go.done.walk` ("You made it to the flag! Great stopping and going.") ends level 3 instead of "Great driving!". Rules are in `logic.ts` (`carTap`, `touchCounts`, `hintOn`, `doneLine`) with four tests; the how-to's level 1 step mentions the car and levels 1–4 gain misses/hints notes. Closes dive gaps 1–6; 7 and 8 remain. Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-20.md#2026-10-20--stop-and-go-every-touch-counts-and-the-hint-on-the-car-slice-1).

## Pile Match levels 1–8 (2026-10-20)

[PR #680](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/680), `claude/pile-match`, built from its [definition](../definitions/pile-match.md) on every default. New game `pile-match` in Counting Cove (now 11 games); preschool 1–4, pre-K 3–6, school 5–8, no toddler. Tap a free piece of a layered heap into a tray; three of a kind pop into the basket until the grass is clear. A 3-slot basket asking for one kind at levels 1–2 (a wrong kind is a miss), a free 5-slot tray from level 3; a full tray costs one miss per episode from level 4; covered touches and put-backs never count, and the two-miss glow plus a wander hint after three put-backs apply at 4–8. Heaps are frozen shapes (`shapes.ts`) with kinds dealt along a clearing route (`logic.ts`); the hint is a memoized search capped at 200,000 states, then the route fallback. The pet counts the pops by threes at levels 6–8. Files: `src/games/pile-match/` (`index`, `logic`, `shapes`, `logic.test`), registry, lands, voice lines `pile.*` and `game.pile-match`, how-to card, `pilematch` browser suite.

Deviations from the definition: logical views are always at least 1024×768, so its portrait usable width of 578 was wrong; the heap always draws at scale 1 and the layout test asserts it at four views. Level 1 always asks apples then shells, but its shape and arrangement are seeded from six flat shapes (no play count exists to author only the first round). The route fallback puts back only tray pieces not of the route's next kind, then takes in route order, which avoids a put-back/take loop. When a pop happens with a full tray, the third piece lands one slot past the tray for the moment before the three fly to the basket.

## Peg Garden: every touch answers, a fair colour level and a true card, slice 1 (2026-10-21)

Branch `claude/peg-garden-every-touch`. On the island the whole screen takes touches (sea beside the board maps to the nearest ledge point, `ledgeX`); couch play keeps the board-sized hit area. Every touch at levels 1–3 makes a rising ring of bubbles at the finger. A touch while a pearl falls never makes a second pearl at levels 2–3 (a soft low pop) and at level 1 queues the next pearl (at most one waiting, never past the six). Level 3 drops with no drift (`driftOf`) and its stall guard runs too (`guided`: after `STALL_AT` = 2 pearls in a row with no new orange bloom, or while the hint is on, the pearl is let go over the `dropAt(…, 0, early)` spot); a guided pearl is never a miss. The hint adds a pulsing yellow ring on the ledge at that spot (`hintX`) beside the arrow, and `hinting` clears after an orange bloom so a later struggle can earn another hint. The ghost finger reads zero drift at 3. Dropped pearls step at a fixed 1/240 s (the ball engine's substep) so play matches `simulate()` at any frame rate (frame jitter had dropped the bot's sure-shot hit rate to 82%). Pixi 8's EventSystem never listens for DOM `pointercancel`, so the game listens on `window` (capture), a cancelled aim fires nothing, and the listener is removed in `destroy()`. The how-to card: level 1 "A tap while a pearl is falling drops the next one", steps 1–3 "Tap anywhere to drop a pearl from above that spot", a level 3 ring line, and the finish per level (1 after six pearls, 2 every flower has bloomed, 3+ the requested flowers). Rule tests: the colour level finishes when the same spot (left edge, middle, beyond right) is tapped every time within 16 pearls; the hinted spot touches a waiting orange bud on 40 boards; the bot's sure shots at the colour level all hit. Closes dive gaps 1–6, 8 and 9. Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-21.md#2026-10-21--peg-garden-every-touch-answers-slice-1).

## Lift It levels 1–6 (2026-10-20)

[PR #692](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/692), `claude/lift-it`, built from its [definition](../definitions/lift-it.md) on every default. Levels: 1 Pull! (prebuilt branch wheel), 2 Push or pull, 3 Too heavy, 4 Twice the rope (knot counter and free knots guess), 5 Who pulls? (the request names ground or ledge pullers), 6 By the numbers (paws and knots cards before the haul).

Accounting: levels 1–4 record no misses. At 5–6 the first tug per rig per parcel on a rig that cannot lift or ends at the wrong pullers is a miss (pressing the greyed "nobody up there" handle counts the same), and a wrong number card is a miss and the right one lights. After a parcel's second miss the best rig's hooks (or the right card) glow, one hint per parcel. "Show a hint" pulls a stroke with her at levels 1–2 (one hint per request, no drawn pet paw: the stroke just happens with a spoken line) and glows at 3–6. One sticker per round; no journal entry. The ghost-finger bot hangs the best rig's wheels, answers cards right and hauls in strokes.

Not built: the definition's replay button (from level 3, replaying the last haul with knots counted aloud) and the drawn pet paw for the level 1–2 hint. The mechanism reviewer is still not named, so speech stays on the ideal rule. Rule tests (18): the rule, working rigs, hauling, authored lines, 500-seed generation per level with no fallback, layout at four views and ledge heights 1–3. Checks: [VERIFICATION](../VERIFICATION.md) 2026-10-20 (Lift It).

New game `fossil-dig` in Tinker Lab (now 12 games), region `tinker-lab`; pre-K 1–2 only; no couch. Files: `src/games/fossil-dig/{index.ts,logic.ts,logic.test.ts}`, the registry, the `tinker-lab` list in `lands.ts`, voice lines, the how-to card, the `fossildig` browser case, slot art and `sfx.brush()`. Brush dust off a stone in one of three dig squares until 70% of the print's cells show (at most twelve pats), tap the flag of the square (red circle, blue triangle, yellow square), then tap the pinned reference leaf and Check. Level 1 matches by outline (star lobed against ribbon); level 2 by edge (saw-star toothed against star smooth) with look-closer badges and a magnifier. Authored first round per level per session (level 1 star in the blue square, level 2 saw-star in the yellow square). A finished round slides the record card into the field notebook and gives one sticker; nothing is saved or filed.

Accounting: a wrong flag and a wrong Check are misses; brushing, early flag, leaf or Check taps (a wiggle and the spoken step), an empty Check, swapping or returning a leaf and the magnifier are free. Two misses on a step glow its answer (one hint per step); "Show a hint" counts at most once per step and during brushing only repeats the words. Start again stops the old continuations and keeps misses and hints. The edge line is spoken only at level 2. The ghost-finger bot plays both levels.

Deviations from the definition's pixels: squares 290x230 landscape and 236x200 portrait with the stone scaled (0.7 / 0.57) inside its square; flags centred above the squares; the record card horizontal in both orientations; the notebook left of the top-right shell button; no separate magnifier badge on the stone; the level-2 hint opens the print's edge only; brush reach is 55 screen units. Rule tests (24): cases, rounds over 500 seeds, dust reveal at most twelve pats, tiling, reach, flags, accounting, layout at four views, two levels and three sites. Checks: [VERIFICATION](../VERIFICATION.md) 2026-10-21 (Fossil Dig).

## Lost Layers levels 1–2 (2026-10-21)

Level 2: a useless look is a miss, Can't tell or a story claim before the look is free ("not yet"), after the look Can't tell or the ruled-out story is a miss. Two misses on a step glow its answer (one hint per step); "Show a hint" counts once per step; restart keeps misses and hints. The ghost-finger bot plays both levels.

## Trackway Tales levels 1–3 (2026-10-21)

Branch `claude/trackway-tales` ([PR #699](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/699)), built from its [definition](../definitions/trackway-tales.md) (slice D5 of the [deep-time study](../deep-dives/subjects/deep-time.md#build-slices-in-order)). New game `trackway-tales`, school 1–3 only, region `tinker-lab`, home land **Story Grove** (now 12 games), nothing saved or filed; no couch. Files: `src/games/trackway-tales/{index.ts,logic.ts,logic.test.ts}`, the registry, `src/content/lands.ts`, voice lines `trackway-tales.*` and `game.trackway-tales`, the how-to card, the `trackwaytales` case in `scripts/browser-check.mjs`. Movement cards with a round Play replay an invented gray mud walker on a clear practice strip above a dried-mud strip (ghost prints, soft thuds; drag the strip to scrub). Tapping a card puts it on the study board, and Check slides its prints onto the mud and lights columns green in order until the first mismatch flashes orange with a spoken kind (extra mark, missing mark, toe the other way). Level 1 walk/hop (4 columns, 2 cards); level 2 which way (6 columns, 3 cards, the magnifier opens on a mark, tapping a mud mark says which way the toes point); level 3 a puddle over columns 3–4 (8 columns, 3 cards plus Can't tell: L3-pause fits two walkers so Can't tell is right; L3-switch is walk-then-hop and Can't tell is a miss). Each level's first round per session is authored (L1-walk with hop then walk; L2-left with column 5 magnified; L3-pause).

Accounting: replays, scrubs, the magnifier, an empty-slot Check and (level 3) a fitting card while another fits are free; a non-fitting Check or a wrong Can't tell is a miss. The second miss lights the glow on the right card, rings the telling column on both strips, replays the answer slowly and counts one hint; "Show a hint" does the same once per round. The ghost-finger bot plays all three levels.

Implementation notes: marks are stored by screen half (a left foot is on the upper half walking right, the lower half walking left), so walk and walk-back differ only by the toes, as the definition's level-2 test requires; single-gait movements carry on to the strip's end (the table's "walk x4 / hop x2" are the four-column picture). Portrait is 1024x1365 logical, so one centred layout serves every view (cards 140x240, Play 104 round).

Deviation from the definition: the home land is Story Grove, not Bubble Beach (decision 2). With ten games Bubble Beach's scenery test (`src/app/scenes/beach.test.ts`, rock pools) fails at school landscape, and Tinker Lab at 13 fails `lab.test.ts`; Barnyard and Story Grove both pass, and Story Grove (Owl Walk, Photo Safari: noticing outdoors) fits better than Barnyard. Rule tests (36): stamping, fitting, mismatch kinds, the puddle, Can't tell right once and wrong once, rounds over 500 seeds, accounting, hints, layout in four views. Checks: [VERIFICATION](../VERIFICATION.md) 2026-10-21 (Trackway Tales).

New game `cut-along` in Painted Canyon (5 games, land id `treehouse`), region `treehouse`, skills fine-motor, shapes and spatial reasoning, music `STYLES.paint`; toddler 1–2, preschool 2–5, pre-K 4–7, school 6–8, no lap level. Snips at 1, a thick line at 2, straight lines at 3, zigzags and waves at 4, curves and a circle at 5, a square and a triangle at 6, picture outlines at 7, and choosing the outline that fits a gap at 8. Reuses Letter Trails' `sampleStroke`, `advanceTrace`, `offTrail`, `showTheWay` and `tuneStep`; rules, corridor, boards and the bot route in `logic.ts`; `sfx.snip()`; `cutalong` browser suite. Every open decision shipped at its default (no Fold & Snip, collage not saved, no lap level, the land's music). Differences from the definition: the way round a closed shape is chosen only once the cut has left the joint by more than 1.5 corridor half-widths (found by the browser check; unit test shown failing on the old rule); the ghost-finger route is thinned to corners plus a point about every 0.15 paper units.

Idea row moved here from the notebook's candidates:

Toddler: tap along thick dashes (snips). Preschool: straight lines, then zigzags and curves. Pre-K: circle, square, triangle. School: **Fold & Snip**: cut notches in folded paper and unfold it to see a symmetric snowflake or heart, later predicting the unfolded result first | Letter Trails' tracer (`advanceTrace`, `traceWaypoints`) already enforces a corridor; straying only pauses the scissors, two strays glow the line, and partial progress is kept. The cut-out can be kept as a treehouse creation | M |

## Little Helpers: every touch answers and the empty crowd shrugs, slice 1 (2026-10-21)

[PR #703](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/703), `claude/little-helpers-every-touch` ([deep dive](../deep-dives/little-helpers.md#build-slices-in-order), gaps 1, 6, 7). A full-screen `field` layer behind the fruit, helpers and whistle sends a helper at level 1; a tap on a waiting helper sends too. While busy (fruit dropping, lifting, praise) any touch makes the nearest helper hop and squeak; at levels 2-6 a sky or grass tap makes the nearest waiting helper wave and chirp. A fruit tap with no helpers left makes the crowd shrug and the pet say `helpers.busy` instead of a boing (never a miss). A whistle with nobody sent is a nudge (`helpers.whistle.empty`), not a miss (decision 2's default). Every fruit of a bunch now goes into the basket (`pileSpot` rows inside the rim). `logic.ts` gained `fieldTouch`, `whistleFor`/`whistleMisses`, `pileSpot` and `BASKET_HALF` with three rule tests; the how-to level-1 step reads "Tap anywhere and a helper runs to carry the fruit home." with notes that every touch is answered and from level 3 only a wrong-team whistle counts. `scripts/browser-check.mjs`: the `batch` helpers case taps the sky at level 1, touches while busy, taps the whistle with nobody sent and the grass (no miss); `woods` helpers level 6 waits for every bunch fruit in the basket. ## Rhythm Neighbors: support truth and a real pool, slice 1 (2026-10-21)

[PR #708](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/708), `claude/rhythm-neighbors-support-truth` ([deep dive](../deep-dives/rhythm-neighbors.md#build-slices-in-order), gaps 1-7 fixed; gap 8 partly: `PLANS` is keyed by level ID and `levels` is a `BandLevels` table, with no `order` yet). `REPLIES` holds twelve distinct shapes: Sound Garden's eight `RHYTHMS` plus LL, SLL, LSL and SLS (the dive listed SLS as an addition to the pool, but it was already in `RHYTHMS`; SLS is the old pool's shape, so SLS is the twelfth). Replies are drawn by length rather than cut: levels 3-4 give 2, 3, 3 notes (one 1-gap shape, then two distinct 2-gap shapes), levels 5-6 give 3, 3, 4. Calls (`CALLS`) are two or three knocks and never the reply's pattern; at 3-4 a call's knock count always differs from the reply's note count. Two-frog replies alternate from a seeded first frog. The teal frog croaks (new `sfx.ribbit(step = -4)`, two short sawtooth bursts gliding down, replacing `sfx.bell(4)`). Every round ends in a joinable song (`finaleEvents`, 4-5 s): the bird knocks its call, every frog on the pond plays the last reply together, her frog taps sound along un-judged (`finale` flag), then `ctx.finish`; level 6's "join a final duet" is now true, with the new voice line `neighbors.duet` replacing `neighbors.done`. Free levels 1-2 end by themselves after eight frog taps (`FREE_TAPS`), the arrow staying for an earlier stop; after six quiet seconds (`IDLE_WIGGLE`) a frog wiggles and croaks once per quiet spell, never counted. At levels 5-6 a gap over 2.5 s (`PAUSE_SECONDS`, `judgeEcho`'s threshold) inside an incomplete reply clears her taps with the line `neighbors.pause` and no miss (decision 2's lean). Extra taps already sounded (the dive said they were silent), but the cap recorded one extra tap, which turned it into a length miss; the cap is now the reply's length, so an extra tap sounds, is not recorded and costs nothing. The how-to finish and note are level-scoped.

Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-21.md#2026-10-21--rhythm-neighbors-support-truth-and-a-real-pool-slice-1).

The thirty-second trot-home goes into the emptier pen only while pens are uneven; once level, idle help wiggles only the bell (a review catch, now guarded and browser-checked). The bot (`fairNeeds`) fills the emptier pen to half each, then rings. Other levels and decisions unchanged.

## Chain Reaction level 9 Two marbles (2026-10-21)

[PR #715](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/715), `claude/chain-reaction-two-marbles`: a red and a blue marble share one 5x4 board (one fixed ramp, two loose ramps, three decoy sockets, one to three accepted designs, two machines per round). Each marble's way down needs its own loose ramp and the two ways never share a cell, so a single ramp cannot serve both; instead the ramp pool is shared and at least one spare socket lies on a marble's way, so a ramp put there for one marble knocks the other off course. Some placement rings one bell while the other misses (both directions), enforced by generation and tested. Cups show a red or blue dot, bells are tinted; red runs, then blue, and `works()` (used by run, hint, bot and solution counting) needs both. A failed run is a free experiment with a red and a blue trail, and tapping either cup replays both; the bulb counts a hint and glows a ramp that matters to the marble that missed. School becomes `{ min 3, max 9, order [3, 4, 5, 6, 9] }`; pre-K is unchanged (the dive's "pre-K 5" is ambiguous when its ladder ends at 3, left as an open choice). Voice `chain.two-start`, `-red`, `-blue`, `-both`, `-rang`; how-to level-9 steps. Known existing quirk: a ramp in the bottom row partly covers the bell beneath it. Checks are in [VERIFICATION](VERIFICATION-2026-10-21.md#2026-10-21--chain-reaction-level-9-two-marbles).

## Pet Kitchen: support truth, slice 1 (2026-10-21)

[PR #716](https://github.com/rjdunlap/neo/pull/716), `claude/pet-kitchen-support-truth` ([deep dive](../deep-dives/pet-kitchen.md#build-slices-in-order), the how-to, first-miss and silent-touch gaps, and the counted serve at 7; IDs, windows and the bot unchanged). `serveTrouble(plates, pieces)` in `logic.ts` names why a share or fraction serve missed: `uncut` (serve before the cut, the instruction repeats; a second uncut miss glows the cut that works), `short` (fewer pieces than friends, such as halves for four at level 2, checked before empty plates; new line `kitchen.short`, and on the second miss only the glow and `kitchen.recut` speak), `empty` (new line `kitchen.empty`) and `unequal` (the existing `kitchen.fair` and `kitchen.fraction-hint`); miss and hint accounting is unchanged. A touch on the uncut food wiggles it and repeats the instruction at most every 3 s, free and never a miss; six seconds of quiet before the cut bounce the cut buttons, and any touch on the counter resets the quiet. Level 7's fair serve counts the pieces aloud while each hops (new line `kitchen.fraction-count`, "{count}. That is {amount}! Thank you, chef!", the count from `countAloud(cut, served)`, such as "one quarter, two quarters, three quarters") and the round finishes after the line. The how-to closes its open ranges (halve step 8-8, pizza step 9-9, notes 7-7 and 8-8, a new 9-9 note that the round arrow puts the pizza back), says at levels 1-4 that touching the food repeats the instruction, and its misses note names a short serve and a friend with less.
